The paper investigates the effectiveness of multi-turn jailbreak attacks on Large Language Models (LLMs). It challenges the conventional understanding that multi-turn attacks are inherently more sophisticated than single-turn attacks. Through empirical analysis using the StrongREJECT benchmark, the authors find that the success of multi-turn attacks can largely be attributed to the attacker's ability to learn from model refusals and retry, essentially resampling single-turn attempts multiple times. The study analyzes several state-of-the-art models, including GPT-4, Claude, and Gemini variants, revealing that jailbreaking success is correlated among similar models and that, counterintuitively, higher reasoning effort in reasoning models often leads to increased attack success. The authors release their source code and suggest that defense efforts should focus on fundamental robustness to harmful requests rather than complex multi-turn pattern detection.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary contribution lies in its finding that multi-turn jailbreak attacks, often perceived as highly sophisticated, are not fundamentally different from multiple single-turn attacks with feedback. This reframing of the problem is novel and challenges existing assumptions in the field. The observation that attack success correlates within model families is also a valuable contribution. However, the finding that increased reasoning can lead to jailbreaks, while interesting, is less novel, as the dual-use nature of knowledge has been previously discussed.

*   **Significance:** The implications of this work are significant for AI safety and evaluation. By demonstrating that simply resampling single-turn attacks can achieve high success rates, the authors highlight a critical vulnerability in current defense mechanisms. This underscores the need for robust defenses against *any* attempt to solicit prohibited content, regardless of the complexity of the interaction. The study provides actionable insights for improving AI safety evaluation methodologies by advocating for systematic resampling to accurately assess model vulnerabilities.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Empirical Rigor:** The paper employs a rigorous empirical methodology, utilizing the StrongREJECT benchmark and evaluating a range of state-of-the-art models.
    *   **Clear and Concise Presentation:** The findings are presented in a clear and concise manner, with effective use of visualizations and statistical analysis.
    *   **Actionable Insights:** The paper offers actionable insights for improving AI safety evaluation and defense mechanisms.
    *   **Open Source:** The release of source code promotes reproducibility and enables further research in this area.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope:** The study primarily focuses on the "Direct Request" tactic. While this is justified by its effectiveness and low false positive rate, it limits the generalizability of the findings to all multi-turn attack strategies.
    *   **Potential Evaluator Bias:** While the authors acknowledge potential inaccuracies in the StrongREJECT evaluator, the potential compounding effect of these errors when testing many attempts is not entirely eliminated.
    *   **Model as Attacker/Evaluator Bias:** The use of GPT-4o-mini for both attack generation and evaluation could lead to some shared bias, potentially artificially inflating measured jailbreak success. While the authors justify this, a separation of models in this role would be ideal in future work.
*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the direction of AI safety research and development. By demonstrating the effectiveness of simple resampling strategies, it may prompt a shift in focus towards building more fundamentally robust defense mechanisms rather than relying on complex multi-turn pattern detection. It's findings also show the need for higher quality single turn defences.
* **Improvements:**
    * Future works could involve analyzing and testing on more sophisticated attacks, such as the Crescendo attack, to ascertain how generalized the findings are, as well as utilizing different models for evaluation.
    * Future works could include use of stronger models as attackers as they may be more capable of generating complex multi-turn attacks, as well as models that are not used as the target system, in order to limit bias from training data that may be correlated with the target models.

**Score: 7.5**

**Justification:** The paper presents a novel and significant finding that challenges conventional wisdom regarding multi-turn jailbreak attacks. The empirical rigor, actionable insights, and open-source release are key strengths. However, the limited scope (focus on "Direct Request," use of a single model for both attack and evaluation), and concerns about evaluator accuracy constrain its overall impact and warrant a slight reduction in the score. The reframing of the problem and the call for fundamentally robust defenses make this a valuable contribution to the field.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### LaRender: Training-Free Occlusion Control in Image Generation via Latent Rendering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07647v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaohang Zhan, Dingming Liu
- **Abstract**: We propose a novel training-free image generation algorithm that precisely controls the occlusion relationships between objects in an image. Existing image generation methods typically rely on prompts to influence occlusion, which often lack precision. While layout-to-image methods provide control over object locations, they fail to address occlusion relationships explicitly. Given a pre-trained image diffusion model, our method leverages volume rendering principles to "render" the scene in latent space, guided by occlusion relationships and the estimated transmittance of objects. This approach does not require retraining or fine-tuning the image diffusion model, yet it enables accurate occlusion control due to its physics-grounded foundation. In extensive experiments, our method significantly outperforms existing approaches in terms of occlusion accuracy. Furthermore, we demonstrate that by adjusting the opacities of objects or concepts during rendering, our method can achieve a variety of effects, such as altering the transparency of objects, the density of mass (e.g., forests), the concentration of particles (e.g., rain, fog), the intensity of light, and the strength of lens effects, etc.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces LaRender, a novel, training-free method for controlling occlusion relationships in image generation. It leverages volume rendering principles within the latent space of a pre-trained image diffusion model. Given an occlusion graph (specifying which objects occlude others) and bounding boxes, the method "renders" the scene by sorting objects, estimating transmittance maps, and fusing latent features based on physical occlusion.  The method doesn't require fine-tuning the diffusion model and enables control over object opacity, producing various visual effects such as altering transparency, fog density, and light intensity.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of applying volume rendering principles in the latent space of a diffusion model for occlusion control is innovative. Existing methods often rely on prompts or layouts, which are less precise. The training-free nature is a significant advantage, as it avoids the need for paired image and occlusion data. The approach is distinctly different from traditional 3D-aware image generation, which often involves GANs or explicit 3D representations. The work's contribution lies in creatively adapting a concept from 3D graphics (volume rendering) to the problem of image generation within the latent space of diffusion models.

*   **Significance:** Precise occlusion control is important for applications requiring accurate spatial arrangements, like advertising or scene generation. LaRender's ability to manipulate opacity and create various visual effects adds value.  The performance, as demonstrated by user studies and metrics like UniDet on the T2I-CompBench++ and RealOcc datasets, outperforms existing methods. While the reported CLIP scores show a minor decrease compared to SDXL, this is a reasonable trade-off for enhanced occlusion control and can likely be addressed with further refinement.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Training-free:** A major advantage, reducing data and computational requirements.
    *   **Precise Occlusion Control:** Outperforms existing approaches, as evidenced by the quantitative and qualitative results.
    *   **Versatility:** Allows for control over opacity and generation of various visual effects.
    *   **Strong empirical validation:** The user studies and quantitative metrics support the claims. The construction of the RealOcc dataset to address limitations of existing datasets (like T2I-CompBench++) is also a strength.
    *   **Physically-grounded approach:** Leverages volume rendering, providing a more principled approach to occlusion.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependence on Layout:** The occlusion results are tied to the quality of the initial layout and bounding box information. Poor layouts can lead to inaccurate occlusion. If the user is bad at creating layout, the result is bad.
    *   **Concept Loss/Mixing:** The paper acknowledges that concepts can sometimes be lost or mixed, indicating that the latent space manipulation is not always perfect and could lead to artifacts.
    *   **Limited Generalization of bounding box from LLMs:** In some cases, the output bounds from LLMs may not be optimal
    *   **Doesn't address precise positioning** The paper itself notes that the method does not address precise positioning, that it instead only addresses occlusion

*   **Potential Impact:** The method can influence research in controllable image generation by offering a new approach to occlusion handling. It might also inspire further exploration of physically-based rendering techniques within latent spaces. The ability to generate diverse visual effects could have commercial applications in content creation.

*   **Rigorous rationale:** Although the core concept is novel and the experimental section validates claims, I am marking the project as only significant. There are a limited number of qualitative examples that are more artistic than realistic. I was unable to evaluate the potential of the impact in many scenarios.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 6/10

### GraphCoT-VLA: A 3D Spatial-Aware Reasoning Vision-Language-Action Model for Robotic Manipulation with Ambiguous Instructions
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07650v1)
- **Authors**: Helong Huang, Min Cen, Kai Tan, Xingyue Quan, Guowei Huang, Hong Zhang
- **Abstract**: Vision-language-action models have emerged as a crucial paradigm in robotic manipulation. However, existing VLA models exhibit notable limitations in handling ambiguous language instructions and unknown environmental states. Furthermore, their perception is largely constrained to static two-dimensional observations, lacking the capability to model three-dimensional interactions between the robot and its environment. To address these challenges, this paper proposes GraphCoT-VLA, an efficient end-to-end model. To enhance the model's ability to interpret ambiguous instructions and improve task planning, we design a structured Chain-of-Thought reasoning module that integrates high-level task understanding and planning, failed task feedback, and low-level imaginative reasoning about future object positions and robot actions. Additionally, we construct a real-time updatable 3D Pose-Object graph, which captures the spatial configuration of robot joints and the topological relationships between objects in 3D space, enabling the model to better understand and manipulate their interactions. We further integrates a dropout hybrid reasoning strategy to achieve efficient control outputs. Experimental results across multiple real-world robotic tasks demonstrate that GraphCoT-VLA significantly outperforms existing methods in terms of task success rate and response speed, exhibiting strong generalization and robustness in open environments and under uncertain instructions.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces GraphCoT-VLA, a vision-language-action model designed for robotic manipulation in scenarios with ambiguous instructions and uncertain environmental states. The model addresses the limitations of existing VLA models, which often struggle with vague commands and rely on static 2D observations. GraphCoT-VLA incorporates two key components: a structured Chain-of-Thought (CoT) module for interpreting ambiguous instructions and improving task planning, and a real-time updatable 3D Pose-Object graph that captures spatial relationships between the robot and objects.  The CoT module integrates task understanding, planning, failure feedback, and imaginative reasoning about future object positions.  The model also employs a dropout-based hybrid reasoning strategy for efficient control. Experimental results demonstrate superior performance in task success rate, response speed, generalization, and robustness compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The combination of a 3D spatial graph with a structured CoT approach appears novel in the context of VLA models for robotic manipulation.  While CoT and spatial reasoning have been explored individually, the integrated approach for handling ambiguity is a key contribution. The introduction of real-time updatable 3D Pose-Object Graph is also innovative, as it captures 3D spatial relationships between the robot and objects.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial gap in existing VLA models: the ability to handle ambiguity and uncertainty in real-world instructions. This is essential for practical applications of robotics in human-robot interaction. The reported improvements in task success rate, response time, generalization, and robustness are promising and could have significant implications for the field.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Well-defined problem and clear motivation.
    *   Novel integration of CoT and 3D spatial reasoning.
    *   Strong experimental results demonstrating improved performance.
    *   Ablation studies that highlight the importance of individual components.
    *   Qualitative visualizations that provide insights into the model's reasoning process.
    *   Explicit modeling of 3D spatial relationships.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited Discussion of Failure Cases: While the paper mentions failure cases of the baseline in the real-world execution section and Fig.4, there is little analysis of GraphCoT-VLA's failure modes or a discussion of situations in which the proposed approach might not perform well.
    *   Lack of statistical significance testing: The paper does not provide any confidence intervals for the metrics reported, nor does it perform statistical significance tests to validate claims of superior performance.
    *   Dependence on external VLM: The method still relies on the capabilities of Qwen2.5-VL, which may have its limitations. The dependency on Qwen2.5-VL may cause GraphCoT-VLA to be limited in some scenarios.
    *   Limited scope: The experiments are restricted to food preparation and outfit selection tasks. While these are relevant, expanding the evaluation to more diverse and complex scenarios would strengthen the paper.
    *   Reliance on future imagination: The paper relies on future imagination to perform robotic manipulation. Future work should be conducted to mitigate the model's reliance on future data.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper could influence the development of more robust and adaptable VLA models for robotics. The emphasis on handling ambiguity and uncertainty aligns with the demands of real-world applications.

**Justification:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of robotic manipulation by addressing a critical challenge of handling ambiguity in instructions. The combination of CoT and 3D spatial reasoning demonstrates a novel approach that yields promising results. However, the limited scope of the experiments, the lack of statistical significance testing, the reliance on a particular VLM and future imagination, and the limited discussion of failure modes constrain the overall impact.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### Understanding Users' Privacy Perceptions Towards LLM's RAG-based Memory
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07664v1)
- **Authors**: Shuning Zhang, Rongjun Ma, Ying Ma, Shixuan Li, Yiqun Xu, Xin Yi, Hewu Li
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly integrating memory functionalities to provide personalized and context-aware interactions. However, user understanding, practices and expectations regarding these memory systems are not yet well understood. This paper presents a thematic analysis of semi-structured interviews with 18 users to explore their mental models of LLM's Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG)-based memory, current usage practices, perceived benefits and drawbacks, privacy concerns and expectations for future memory systems. Our findings reveal diverse and often incomplete mental models of how memory operates. While users appreciate the potential for enhanced personalization and efficiency, significant concerns exist regarding privacy, control and the accuracy of remembered information. Users express a desire for granular control over memory generation, management, usage and updating, including clear mechanisms for reviewing, editing, deleting and categorizing memories, as well as transparent insight into how memories and inferred information are used. We discuss design implications for creating more user-centric, transparent, and trustworthy LLM memory systems.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper explores users' understanding and privacy perceptions related to Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG)-based memory in Large Language Models (LLMs). It presents a thematic analysis of interviews with 18 participants, examining their mental models of LLM memory, current practices, perceived benefits and drawbacks, privacy concerns, and expectations for future memory systems. The findings reveal diverse and often incomplete mental models, highlighting concerns about privacy, control, and accuracy of remembered information. Users desire granular control, transparency, and mechanisms for managing their data. The paper concludes with design implications for creating more user-centric and trustworthy LLM memory systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The paper addresses an important and timely issue: user perceptions of memory in LLMs.  While research exists on privacy in LLMs and user concerns in general, it is valuable to focus specifically on RAG-based memory, which introduces unique challenges related to persistent data storage and potential misuse.  The exploration of mental models, privacy calculus, and design implications strengthens its novelty. It differentiates itself from more purely technical works or general HCI studies about privacy.

**Significance:** The paper makes a significant contribution by providing empirical data on how users perceive LLM memory, which is a rapidly evolving area. It uncovers gaps in user understanding and highlights areas of concern, such as data leakage and epistemological certainty.  The detailed exploration of users' protective strategies and the identified challenges provide valuable insights for designing more user-centered and trustworthy LLM memory systems.  The design implications are actionable and offer a practical roadmap for future research and development.

**Strengths:**

*   **Empirical grounding:** The use of interviews provides rich, qualitative data that captures the nuances of user perceptions.
*   **Focus on RAG-based memory:**  Concentrating on RAG-based memory systems provides a specific and relevant contribution to the field.
*   **Detailed analysis:** The thematic analysis is thorough and well-structured, presenting clear mental models, privacy calculus factors, and user challenges.
*   **Actionable design implications:** The paper concludes with concrete design suggestions that can inform the development of future LLM memory systems.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited sample size:** While the interviews are in-depth, a sample size of 18 participants is relatively small and may not be representative of all LLM users. The participants are all Chinese, so this limits the generalizability of the findings to other cultures.
*   **ChatGPT-centric interviews:** Basing the interviews around ChatGPT's memory feature may introduce bias, as participants' perceptions may be influenced by their experience with this specific platform.
*   **Lack of generalizability:**  The study's findings may not be directly transferable to other LLM architectures or memory implementations beyond RAG. Further investigation is needed to determine how these results might extend to different regions.
*  **Limited critical reflection on methodology:** A stronger and more explicit discussion about the limitations of thematic analysis and potential biases could strengthen the study.

**Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of LLM memory systems by advocating for greater transparency, control, and user-centricity. It can also stimulate further research on user perceptions of AI and the ethical implications of memory integration in LLMs. As the widespread adoption of LLMs continues to increase, it is very important to provide more transparency for the data management.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper addresses an important and timely issue, provides empirical data on user perceptions, and offers actionable design implications. However, the limitations related to sample size, cultural specificity, platform bias, and the lack of methodological reflexivity limit the overall impact and influence. Therefore, I assign the score.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### 1-2-3 Check: Enhancing Contextual Privacy in LLM via Multi-Agent Reasoning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07667v1)
- **Authors**: Wenkai Li, Liwen Sun, Zhenxiang Guan, Xuhui Zhou, Maarten Sap
- **Abstract**: Addressing contextual privacy concerns remains challenging in interactive settings where large language models (LLMs) process information from multiple sources (e.g., summarizing meetings with private and public information). We introduce a multi-agent framework that decomposes privacy reasoning into specialized subtasks (extraction, classification), reducing the information load on any single agent while enabling iterative validation and more reliable adherence to contextual privacy norms. To understand how privacy errors emerge and propagate, we conduct a systematic ablation over information-flow topologies, revealing when and why upstream detection mistakes cascade into downstream leakage. Experiments on the ConfAIde and PrivacyLens benchmark with several open-source and closed-sourced LLMs demonstrate that our best multi-agent configuration substantially reduces private information leakage (\textbf{18\%} on ConfAIde and \textbf{19\%} on PrivacyLens with GPT-4o) while preserving the fidelity of public content, outperforming single-agent baselines. These results highlight the promise of principled information-flow design in multi-agent systems for contextual privacy with LLMs.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and critical evaluation of the paper "1-2-3 Check: Enhancing Contextual Privacy in LLM via Multi-Agent Reasoning":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of maintaining contextual privacy in Large Language Models (LLMs) when processing information from multiple sources. It introduces a multi-agent framework that decomposes privacy reasoning into specialized tasks (extraction, classification, verification) handled by distinct agents (Extractor, Executor, Checker). This design aims to mitigate cognitive overload, improve adherence to privacy norms, and facilitate iterative validation. The authors conduct ablation studies over various information flow topologies to understand how errors propagate and impact privacy. Experiments on ConfAIde and PrivacyLens benchmarks demonstrate that the multi-agent configuration reduces private information leakage compared to single-agent baselines, while preserving the fidelity of public content.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using a multi-agent approach for contextual privacy is reasonably novel, particularly in the context of LLMs. Prior multi-agent work has not focused on the nuanced issue of information flow and its direct impact on both privacy and utility. Decomposing the privacy reasoning process into distinct roles (Extractor, Executor, Checker) and analyzing various information sharing strategies between these agents is a valuable contribution.

*   **Significance:** Contextual privacy is a growing concern as LLMs are integrated into real-world applications. The paper addresses a critical gap: the limitations of single-agent, single-prompt mechanisms for handling nuanced privacy norms. The demonstrated reductions in private information leakage on established benchmarks (ConfAIde and PrivacyLens) are significant and highlight the potential of multi-agent systems in this domain. The systematic ablation studies provide valuable insights into error propagation and trade-offs between privacy and utility, which can inform future system design. The analysis of withholding and providing meeting transcripts shows an impact in the agents collaboration process.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The paper clearly articulates the problem and motivates the multi-agent approach based on the limitations of existing single-agent solutions.
    *   The proposed framework is well-defined and modular, with clear roles for each agent.
    *   The ablation studies are comprehensive and provide valuable insights into the impact of information flow on privacy and utility.
    *   The experimental results on ConfAIde and PrivacyLens demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed approach in reducing privacy leakage while preserving content fidelity.
    *   The paper includes a detailed error analysis and identifies common failure modes, providing actionable insights for robust system design.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper acknowledges limitations regarding increased computational overhead and integration complexity, which are inherent to multi-agent systems. While improvements were achieved, they might not translate to real world scenarios.
    *   The domain generalization remains a concern, as the Extractor's event schema and the Checker's privacy rules need to be hand-crafted for each application domain.
    *   The analysis focuses primarily on explicit event classification and does not fully address the potential for inference-based privacy leaks.
    *   While the paper mentions several LLM backbones used for validation, there appears to be limited validation using other more efficient and open source models.
    *   The experimental setups primarily use a read-only Executor. It remains to be proven if in real world applications of multi-agent workflows, different scenarios will benefit from such constraints.
    *   The paper could benefit from a more detailed discussion of the ethical implications of using LLMs for privacy-sensitive tasks, particularly regarding the potential for bias and discrimination.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of privacy-preserving LLM applications by demonstrating the benefits of multi-agent reasoning and principled information flow. The insights from the ablation studies and error analysis can guide the development of more robust and reliable privacy solutions. Future research can build upon this work by addressing the limitations regarding domain generalization, inference-based leaks, and computational overhead.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of contextual privacy in LLMs. The multi-agent approach and systematic analysis provide valuable insights and demonstrate improved performance compared to single-agent baselines. However, the limitations regarding computational overhead, domain generalization, and the handling of inference-based leaks prevent the paper from being truly exceptional. While the results are promising, further research is needed to address these limitations and translate the findings into practical applications.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 7/10

### Semantic Caching for Low-Cost LLM Serving: From Offline Learning to Online Adaptation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07675v1)
- **Authors**: Xutong Liu, Baran Atalar, Xiangxiang Dai, Jinhang Zuo, Siwei Wang, John C. S. Lui, Wei Chen, Carlee Joe-Wong
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are revolutionizing how users interact with information systems, yet their high inference cost poses serious scalability and sustainability challenges. Caching inference responses, allowing them to be retrieved without another forward pass through the LLM, has emerged as one possible solution. Traditional exact-match caching, however, overlooks the semantic similarity between queries, leading to unnecessary recomputation. Semantic caching addresses this by retrieving responses based on semantic similarity, but introduces a fundamentally different cache eviction problem: one must account for mismatch costs between incoming queries and cached responses. Moreover, key system parameters, such as query arrival probabilities and serving costs, are often unknown and must be learned over time. Existing semantic caching methods are largely ad-hoc, lacking theoretical foundations and unable to adapt to real-world uncertainty. In this paper, we present a principled, learning-based framework for semantic cache eviction under unknown query and cost distributions. We formulate both offline optimization and online learning variants of the problem, and develop provably efficient algorithms with state-of-the-art guarantees. We also evaluate our framework on a synthetic dataset, showing that our proposed algorithms perform matching or superior performance compared with baselines.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Semantic Caching for Low-Cost LLM Serving: From Offline Learning to Online Adaptation":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the challenge of reducing the high inference cost of serving Large Language Models (LLMs) through semantic caching. It proposes a principled, learning-based framework for semantic cache eviction under uncertainty, where query arrival probabilities and serving costs are unknown. The framework generalizes exact-match caching by incorporating a mismatch cost for semantically similar queries. It offers solutions for three settings: the oracle setting (known parameters), offline learning (parameters learned from a static dataset), and online adaptive learning (real-time learning from partial feedback). The authors provide algorithms with provable guarantees for each setting, including Reverse Greedy, CUCB-SC, and CLCB-SC-LS, with the latter specifically designed for low switching costs. Experiments on synthetic datasets demonstrate improved performance compared to baselines.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents a novel framework for semantic caching that considers both offline learning and online adaptation with uncertainty. While semantic caching itself isn't entirely new (GPTCache and related works exist), the rigorous theoretical treatment and the focus on online learning with limited cache switches is significant. The modeling of mismatch cost and the systematic treatment of the three uncertainty settings (oracle, offline, online) offer incremental novelty compared to existing heuristic approaches. The provable performance guarantees, particularly in the online setting with low switching costs, are also novel and valuable.
*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical problem in LLM deployment: reducing inference costs for scalability and sustainability.  The proposed solutions have the potential to make LLM services more accessible and efficient. The theoretical guarantees are important for understanding the performance of semantic caching at scale and for guiding system optimization. The online adaptive learning approach is particularly relevant for real-world scenarios where query patterns and costs change over time.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Principled Approach:** The paper provides a rigorous theoretical framework for semantic caching, moving beyond ad-hoc methods.
    *   **Comprehensive Treatment:** The framework considers different levels of uncertainty, offering solutions for various deployment scenarios.
    *   **Provable Guarantees:**  The paper presents algorithms with state-of-the-art performance guarantees for each setting.
    *   **Practical Algorithms:** The proposed algorithms are designed to be efficient and adaptable to real-world constraints like limited cache switches.
    *   **Empirical Validation:** The experiments demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed algorithms compared to baselines.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Synthetic Datasets:** The experiments are primarily conducted on synthetic datasets. Evaluating the framework on real-world datasets with complex query patterns and diverse semantic relationships is crucial for assessing its practical applicability.
    *   **Simplified Cost Model:** The cost model assumes a sub-Gaussian noise for query serving, whereas the mismatch cost parameter *gamma* is simply assumed equal to 1, and isn't optimized. These are likely simplifications that might not hold in all real-world scenarios, potentially limiting the accuracy of the model.
    *   **Scalability in High Dimensions:** Embedding high-dimensional queries for real world LLMs at scale remains a computationally difficult task that warrants additional detail.
    *   **Limited Ablation:** While there's some ablation (especially regarding cache size), deeper ablation studies on specific components of the algorithms (e.g., the LCB component in CLCB-SC-LS) could further strengthen the understanding of their individual contributions.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of future LLM caching systems.  The theoretical framework and algorithms provide a foundation for further research on semantic caching under uncertainty. The online adaptive learning approach, in particular, is likely to be relevant for real-world deployments. The insights on balancing exploration and exploitation in the context of limited cache switches could also be valuable for other online learning problems in LLM serving.

*Score: 7*

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field of LLM serving by providing a theoretically sound and practically relevant framework for semantic caching under uncertainty. The novelty lies primarily in the comprehensive treatment of different uncertainty settings and the development of algorithms with provable guarantees, particularly for online learning with low switching costs. The paper's strength lies in its principled approach and comprehensive analysis. However, the use of synthetic datasets and simplified cost models limits the assessment of its practical applicability. A score of 7 reflects the paper's significant contribution, balanced by the limitations in the empirical evaluation and assumptions in the cost model. While significant, the jump to a higher score would need demonstration of the approaches working robustly and effectively across a wide array of *real-world* deployments and use cases.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### DiffVC-OSD: One-Step Diffusion-based Perceptual Neural Video Compression Framework
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07682v1)
- **Authors**: Wenzhuo Ma, Zhenzhong Chen
- **Abstract**: In this work, we first propose DiffVC-OSD, a One-Step Diffusion-based Perceptual Neural Video Compression framework. Unlike conventional multi-step diffusion-based methods, DiffVC-OSD feeds the reconstructed latent representation directly into a One-Step Diffusion Model, enhancing perceptual quality through a single diffusion step guided by both temporal context and the latent itself. To better leverage temporal dependencies, we design a Temporal Context Adapter that encodes conditional inputs into multi-level features, offering more fine-grained guidance for the Denoising Unet. Additionally, we employ an End-to-End Finetuning strategy to improve overall compression performance. Extensive experiments demonstrate that DiffVC-OSD achieves state-of-the-art perceptual compression performance, offers about 20$\times$ faster decoding and a 86.92\% bitrate reduction compared to the corresponding multi-step diffusion-based variant.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "DiffVC-OSD: One-Step Diffusion-based Perceptual Neural Video Compression Framework."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DiffVC-OSD, a novel neural video compression framework that leverages a one-step diffusion model to achieve state-of-the-art perceptual compression performance. Unlike existing multi-step diffusion-based approaches, DiffVC-OSD feeds the reconstructed latent representation directly into a one-step diffusion model, enhancing perceptual quality while significantly reducing decoding time. The framework also incorporates a Temporal Context Adapter (TCA) to better utilize temporal dependencies and employs an end-to-end finetuning strategy to optimize rate-distortion-perception trade-off. Experiments demonstrate that DiffVC-OSD achieves superior perceptual compression performance, a significant bitrate reduction, and faster decoding compared to multi-step diffusion-based alternatives.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the application of a *one-step* diffusion model within a video compression framework. This is a departure from the more common multi-step diffusion processes. The introduction of the Temporal Context Adapter (TCA) for fine-grained temporal context integration is another contributing factor to the paper's novelty. While leveraging diffusion models for video compression is not entirely new, the specific architecture and the training strategies employed present an advance in efficient perceptual coding.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its ability to address the limitations of multi-step diffusion-based video compression: high computational cost, slow decoding, and difficulty in end-to-end optimization. DiffVC-OSD offers a more practical approach by drastically reducing the number of diffusion steps, leading to significant performance benefits in terms of both bitrate and decoding speed. This makes perceptual video compression more accessible for real-time applications. The substantial (86.92%) bitrate reduction and 20x speed-up are valuable improvements. State-of-the-art results, if robust across datasets, also signify a practical contribution.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **One-Step Diffusion:** This is the core strength, addressing the high computational cost of multi-step methods.
    *   **Temporal Context Adapter (TCA):** A well-engineered component for enhancing temporal dependency exploitation.
    *   **End-to-End Finetuning:** Allows for a global optimization of the model, leading to better rate-distortion-perception trade-offs.
    *   **Significant Performance Gains:** The paper reports substantial improvements in bitrate reduction and decoding speed, which are practically relevant.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is generally well-written and easy to understand, with clear explanations of the proposed architecture and training strategy.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on Pre-trained Diffusion Models:** The framework relies on a pre-trained Stable Diffusion model. While this is common, the performance is tied to the capabilities of this pre-trained model. Future work could explore adapting or training diffusion models explicitly for video compression.
    *   **Complexity of the Architecture:** While the components are well-explained, the overall architecture remains relatively complex with many modules that may be difficult for others to replicate. More simplification could be investigated.
    *   **Limited Ablation Study:** While an ablation study is present, it could be more extensive. Specifically, understanding the individual contribution of each component to performance is crucial, and additional ablations may provide further insights.
    *   **Dependency on DiffVC:** The paper builds upon DiffVC [3]. While acknowledging this as a foundation is commendable, a stronger emphasis on what is drastically different beyond the one-step aspect should be highlighted.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work has the potential to influence the field by pushing the boundaries of efficient perceptual video compression. The one-step diffusion approach could inspire new research directions in this area, particularly exploring different architectural designs and training strategies for diffusion models tailored for compression tasks.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper presents a significant contribution to the field of neural video compression by introducing a practically viable one-step diffusion model. The improvements in bitrate and decoding speed are substantial and address key limitations of previous multi-step approaches. The TCA and end-to-end finetuning further enhance the performance of the framework. While the reliance on pre-trained diffusion models and the architecture's complexity pose certain limitations, the overall impact of the work is considerable. Because this improves upon an existing architecture and is limited by the already powerful Diffusion Model, it isn't groundbreaking.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: eess.IV
- **Score**: 7/10

### LoSemB: Logic-Guided Semantic Bridging for Inductive Tool Retrieval
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07690v1)
- **Authors**: Luyao Zhuang, Qinggang Zhang, Huachi Zhou, Juhua Liu, Qing Li, Xiao Huang
- **Abstract**: Tool learning has emerged as a promising paradigm for large language models (LLMs) to solve many real-world tasks. Nonetheless, with the tool repository rapidly expanding, it is impractical to contain all tools within the limited input length of LLMs. To alleviate these issues, researchers have explored incorporating a tool retrieval module to select the most relevant tools or represent tools as unique tokens within LLM parameters. However, most state-of-the-art methods are under transductive settings, assuming all tools have been observed during training. Such a setting deviates from reality as the real-world tool repository is evolving and incorporates new tools frequently. When dealing with these unseen tools, which refer to tools not encountered during the training phase, these methods are limited by two key issues, including the large distribution shift and the vulnerability of similarity-based retrieval. To this end, inspired by human cognitive processes of mastering unseen tools through discovering and applying the logical information from prior experience, we introduce a novel Logic-Guided Semantic Bridging framework for inductive tool retrieval, namely, LoSemB, which aims to mine and transfer latent logical information for inductive tool retrieval without costly retraining. Specifically, LoSemB contains a logic-based embedding alignment module to mitigate distribution shifts and implements a relational augmented retrieval mechanism to reduce the vulnerability of similarity-based retrieval. Extensive experiments demonstrate that LoSemB achieves advanced performance in inductive settings while maintaining desirable effectiveness in the transductive setting.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the LoSemB paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of inductive tool retrieval for Large Language Models (LLMs). Traditional tool retrieval methods, which involve selecting relevant tools from a repository for LLMs to use, often struggle when faced with "unseen" tools (i.e., tools not present during the model's training). The paper identifies two key challenges: (1) a large distribution shift between seen and unseen tools and (2) the vulnerability of similarity-based retrieval methods which are sensitive to the quality of representations and can lead to errors when tools have similar descriptions but different functionalities.

To overcome these issues, the authors propose LoSemB (Logic-Guided Semantic Bridging), a novel framework that leverages logical information extracted from a knowledge graph of tools and instructions. LoSemB employs a logic-based embedding alignment module to mitigate the distribution shift and a relational augmented retrieval mechanism to improve retrieval accuracy.  Experimental results demonstrate that LoSemB performs significantly better than existing methods in inductive settings while maintaining competitive performance in transductive settings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The key novelty of this paper lies in its focus on *inductive* tool retrieval and its utilization of logical information derived from tool co-occurrence and instruction-tool interaction patterns to address the challenges of unseen tools. Most prior work assumes a transductive setting. The idea of drawing inspiration from human cognitive processes (leveraging prior experience to understand new tools) is well-motivated. The architecture, particularly the logic-based embedding alignment, is a novel contribution.
*   **Significance:** The paper tackles a practically important problem. As LLMs are increasingly used in real-world scenarios, their ability to adapt to dynamically changing tool repositories is crucial. Addressing the limitations of current methods in handling unseen tools is a significant step toward making tool-augmented LLMs more robust and versatile. By making it easier to add new tools to LLMs, the paper has the potential to make tool-augmented LLMs more versatile and adaptable. The performance improvements shown in the inductive setting are substantial, demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed approach.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clearly defines and motivates the problem of inductive tool retrieval.
    *   Identifies and analyzes the limitations of existing retrieval methods in the context of unseen tools.
    *   Proposes a novel and well-designed framework (LoSemB) with two key components.
    *   Provides thorough experimental results demonstrating the effectiveness of LoSemB in both inductive and transductive settings.
    *   Includes ablation studies to analyze the contribution of each component of LoSemB.
    *   Provides a case study to illustrate the benefits of LoSemB in practical scenarios.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The reliance on constructing a logical graph might be a limitation, as it requires curating tool-instruction interactions. While the paper shows good results on ToolBench, the performance might depend on the quality and completeness of the graph data. The robustness of the approach to noisy or incomplete logical graphs could be discussed further.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the direction of research in tool learning and LLM augmentation. By highlighting the importance of inductive tool retrieval and demonstrating a practical solution, it could encourage researchers to focus on developing more robust and adaptable tool retrieval methods. The proposed architecture, particularly the logic-based embedding alignment, could serve as a basis for future research in this area. The concepts may be generalized to other retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) tasks.

*   **Justification for Score:** Given the novelty of the problem formulation (inductive tool retrieval), the well-motivated and well-engineered solution, and the substantial experimental results demonstrating improved performance, I assign a score of 8. The strengths of the paper outweigh the weaknesses, and it offers a significant contribution to the field. While the reliance on a logical graph is a potential limitation, it is addressed through the proposed architecture and empirical results.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 8/10

### Semantic-Enhanced Time-Series Forecasting via Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07697v1)
- **Authors**: Hao Liu, Chun Yang, Zhang xiaoxing, Xiaobin Zhu
- **Abstract**: Time series forecasting plays a significant role in finance, energy, meteorology, and IoT applications. Recent studies have leveraged the generalization capabilities of large language models (LLMs) to adapt to time series forecasting, achieving promising performance. However, existing studies focus on token-level modal alignment, instead of bridging the intrinsic modality gap between linguistic knowledge structures and time series data patterns, greatly limiting the semantic representation. To address this issue, we propose a novel Semantic-Enhanced LLM (SE-LLM) that explores the inherent periodicity and anomalous characteristics of time series to embed into the semantic space to enhance the token embedding. This process enhances the interpretability of tokens for LLMs, thereby activating the potential of LLMs for temporal sequence analysis. Moreover, existing Transformer-based LLMs excel at capturing long-range dependencies but are weak at modeling short-term anomalies in time-series data. Hence, we propose a plugin module embedded within self-attention that models long-term and short-term dependencies to effectively adapt LLMs to time-series analysis. Our approach freezes the LLM and reduces the sequence dimensionality of tokens, greatly reducing computational consumption. Experiments demonstrate the superiority performance of our SE-LLM against the state-of-the-art (SOTA) methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes Semantic-Enhanced LLM (SE-LLM), a novel framework for time series forecasting using large language models (LLMs). The core idea is to bridge the modality gap between linguistic knowledge structures and time series data patterns, which the authors argue existing methods fail to adequately address. SE-LLM consists of two primary components:

1.  **Temporal-Semantic Cross-Correlation (TSCC) Module:**  This module explores the inherent periodicity and anomalous characteristics of time series to embed them into the semantic space, enhancing token interpretability for LLMs. It aligns temporal embeddings with the semantic space and uses a Variational Autoencoder (VAE) to capture statistical characteristics and decompose anomaly/de-anomaly components.

2.  **Time-Adapter:**  This module is a plugin embedded within the self-attention mechanism to model both long-term and short-term dependencies in time-series data. It comprises dual linear layers and parallel LSTM units to capture temporal dependencies.

The authors freeze the LLM and reduce sequence dimensionality to reduce computational consumption. They evaluate SE-LLM against SOTA methods on various time series datasets, demonstrating superior performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of explicitly bridging the gap between temporal patterns and semantic space by embedding time series characteristics into the token embeddings is novel. Existing LLM-based approaches often focus on token-level alignment or converting temporal information into textual prompts, which this work argues can be suboptimal. The TSCC module and Time-Adapter are both well-motivated and potentially impactful contributions.
*   **Significance:** Time series forecasting is a crucial area, and enhancing the performance of LLMs in this domain is significant. The proposed SE-LLM framework aims to improve interpretability and performance by addressing the modality gap. The experiments show promising results against SOTA methods.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Motivation:** The paper clearly identifies a limitation in existing LLM-based time series forecasting approaches, which mainly focuses on token level modality allignment or the use of textual descriptions and the associated modality gap.
    *   **Well-Defined Components:**  The TSCC module and Time-Adapter are well-defined and contribute to the overall framework. The incorporation of a VAE for anomaly detection is a sensible choice for enhancing semantic information.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The paper includes extensive experiments on various datasets, comparing SE-LLM against a wide range of baselines.  The ablation studies further validate the contributions of each module.
    *   **Focus on Efficiency:** The authors address computational efficiency by freezing the LLM and reducing sequence dimensionality.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited LLM Exploration:** The paper primarily uses GPT-2 and studies a limited set of lightweight LLMs. Evaluating SE-LLM with larger and more recent LLMs (e.g., those mentioned in the paper like Qwen or DeepSeek) would strengthen the results.
    *   **Qualitative Analysis Depth:**  While a qualitative analysis is included, a deeper dive into *why* TSCC improves specific predictions would be beneficial.  Visualizations of the learned embeddings and how they change with the module's presence could further enhance understanding. The current visualization results are not particularly insightful.
    *   **Ablation Study Design:** Although conducted, the full benefits of each module in more complicated scenarios could be further emphasised.

*   **Impact:** The SE-LLM framework can potentially influence future research in LLM-based time series forecasting by providing a new perspective on bridging the modality gap. The TSCC module and Time-Adapter can be adopted or adapted by other researchers in the field. The framework also has potential applications in various domains, such as finance, energy, and meteorology.

**Justification of Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses of the paper, a score of 7 is appropriate. The paper addresses a real problem in LLM-based time series forecasting, presents a novel approach with well-defined components, and provides compelling experimental results. The weaknesses, such as limited LLM exploration and depth of qualitative analysis, limit the impact somewhat, but the overall contribution is significant enough to warrant a score of 7.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### Make Your MoVe: Make Your 3D Contents by Adapting Multi-View Diffusion Models to External Editing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07700v1)
- **Authors**: Weitao Wang, Haoran Xu, Jun Meng, Haoqian Wang
- **Abstract**: As 3D generation techniques continue to flourish, the demand for generating personalized content is rapidly rising. Users increasingly seek to apply various editing methods to polish generated 3D content, aiming to enhance its color, style, and lighting without compromising the underlying geometry. However, most existing editing tools focus on the 2D domain, and directly feeding their results into 3D generation methods (like multi-view diffusion models) will introduce information loss, degrading the quality of the final 3D assets. In this paper, we propose a tuning-free, plug-and-play scheme that aligns edited assets with their original geometry in a single inference run. Central to our approach is a geometry preservation module that guides the edited multi-view generation with original input normal latents. Besides, an injection switcher is proposed to deliberately control the supervision extent of the original normals, ensuring the alignment between the edited color and normal views. Extensive experiments show that our method consistently improves both the multi-view consistency and mesh quality of edited 3D assets, across multiple combinations of multi-view diffusion models and editing methods.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "Make Your Move: Make Your 3D Contents by Adapting Multi-View Diffusion Models to External Editing":

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the problem of adapting multi-view diffusion models for 3D content generation after an initial 2D editing step. The authors observe that directly feeding edited 2D images into multi-view diffusion models often leads to geometric inconsistencies and artifacts in the generated 3D content. To overcome this, they propose a tuning-free, plug-and-play scheme called "Make Your MoVe" (MyMV). The key components of their approach are:

1.  **Parallel Dual-Pipeline:** Processing both the original and edited images in parallel.
2.  **Geometry Preservation Module:**  Supervising the edited image generation using the normal maps derived from the original image, ensuring geometric consistency.
3.  **Injection Switcher:**  Controlling the extent to which the original normal maps are injected during the diffusion process, allowing for a balance between geometry preservation and incorporating the intended color and texture edits.

The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of their method through experiments combining several multi-view diffusion models (Wonder3D, Era3D) with various 2D editing techniques (style transfer, prompt instruction, relighting). They show improved multi-view consistency and mesh quality compared to naively applying existing 2D edits and 3D generation methods.  They also perform quantitative evaluations and user studies to support their claims.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:**

The novelty of the paper lies primarily in the architecture designed to address the specific challenge of integrating 2D edits with multi-view diffusion models. The idea of using the original image's normal maps to guide the diffusion process of the edited image is a good intuition.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The problem is well-motivated and relevant, as users increasingly want to customize 3D content with editing tools.
    *   The geometry preservation module, especially the injection switcher, provides a way to balance appearance editing and geometric consistency, a crucial aspect that's been overlooked in previous work. The design of injection switcher adds a clever control mechanism.
    *   The method is presented as a plug-and-play solution, enhancing its usability. It is important since it can be applied without re-training any of the networks involved.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The individual components, while combined in a novel way, are not inherently revolutionary.  Normal map supervision has been used in other contexts, and attention mechanisms in diffusion models are already widespread.
    *   The paper could better articulate how the interplay between the components is uniquely designed for *this* specific problem (adapting multi-view diffusion to external editing). What prevents the geometry preservation from interfering with the texture edits? A more technical analysis of the impact of the parameters λ and *ts* would be interesting to the reader.
    *   Although plug-and-play is claimed, a grid search over hyperparameters (λ, ts) for optimal results suggests that the method may require parameter tuning for optimal results.

**Significance:**

The significance of the paper lies in its potential to improve the workflow for creating personalized 3D content. By offering a method to integrate 2D editing tools with multi-view diffusion models, it opens up new possibilities for artists and designers.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The experiments are comprehensive, covering multiple diffusion models and editing techniques.
    *   The user study provides valuable feedback on the perceived quality of the generated 3D content. It is a better way of evaluating the results of the algorithm rather than CLIP-similarity, which is more of an implementation detail.
    *   The method produces visually appealing results, demonstrating the practicality of the approach.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The quantitative evaluation, particularly CLIP Similarity, may not perfectly capture the nuances of geometric accuracy and artistic merit. More specific geometric metrics could strengthen the evaluation.
    *   Comparison to Style3D is weak because it simply references qualitative results from the original paper instead of conducting a direct, controlled comparison.
    *   The improvements over "naive" combinations of existing methods are good, but the overall benefit should be compared with similar state-of-the-art research from the current year.

**Justification of Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7** to this paper.

**Rationale:**

The paper addresses a practically important problem and proposes a reasonable solution that combines existing techniques in a novel architecture. The results show tangible improvements over baseline methods. However, the novelty is incremental rather than groundbreaking, and the evaluation could be strengthened with more focused geometric metrics and a direct, controlled comparison to Style3D. While the plug-and-play nature is appealing, the hyperparameter tuning requirements need to be acknowledged clearly. The significance lies in improving a specific workflow for 3D content creation, which can have a positive impact on the artistic and design communities.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 0/10

### What am I missing here?: Evaluating Large Language Models for Masked Sentence Prediction
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07702v1)
- **Authors**: Charlie Wyatt, Aditya Joshi, Flora Salim
- **Abstract**: Transformer-based models primarily rely on Next Token Prediction (NTP), which predicts the next token in a sequence based on the preceding context. However, NTP's focus on single-token prediction often limits a model's ability to plan ahead or maintain long-range coherence, raising questions about how well LLMs can predict longer contexts, such as full sentences within structured documents. While NTP encourages local fluency, it provides no explicit incentive to ensure global coherence across sentence boundaries-an essential skill for reconstructive or discursive tasks. To investigate this, we evaluate three commercial LLMs (GPT-4o, Claude 3.5 Sonnet, and Gemini 2.0 Flash) on Masked Sentence Prediction (MSP) - the task of infilling a randomly removed sentence - from three domains: ROCStories (narrative), Recipe1M (procedural), and Wikipedia (expository). We assess both fidelity (similarity to the original sentence) and cohesiveness (fit within the surrounding context). Our key finding reveals that commercial LLMs, despite their superlative performance in other tasks, are poor at predicting masked sentences in low-structured domains, highlighting a gap in current model capabilities.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper evaluates how well large language models (LLMs) perform on the task of Masked Sentence Prediction (MSP), where the goal is to fill in a missing sentence within a given document context. The authors test three commercial LLMs (GPT-40, Claude 3.5 Sonnet, and Gemini 2.0 Flash) on three different domains: narrative (ROCStories), procedural (Recipe1M), and expository (Wikipedia).  They assess both the fidelity (similarity to the original sentence) and cohesiveness (fit within the surrounding context) of the generated sentences. The key finding is that despite strong performance in other tasks, these LLMs struggle with MSP, especially in low-structured domains. Fidelity is generally low, and while human annotators sometimes find the outputs contextually appropriate, the models exhibit limitations in document-level understanding. The paper highlights a potential gap in current LLM capabilities, raising concerns about applying them in tasks requiring factual accuracy and precise reconstruction.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a relevant and important question about LLMs: how well do they understand and model sentence-level structure within broader document context, rather than merely excelling at next token prediction? While MSP itself isn't a brand new task, the *specific application to evaluate commercial LLMs out-of-the-box* and the *focus on fidelity vs. cohesion* is valuable and highlights an important practical limitation. Prior research often involves fine-tuning, but this paper takes the valuable approach of assessing the models as they are, and the authors convincingly explain how this study fills a critical gap in research.

*   **Significance:**  The findings have practical implications for using LLMs in applications such as summarization, document editing, and content generation where sentence-level understanding and global coherence are crucial.  The observation that LLMs prioritize plausibility over fidelity raises concerns about their use in high-stakes domains requiring factual accuracy, such as legal or historical documents. The distinction between fidelity and cohesion and the finding that structured domains lead to higher fidelity but can mask problems of plausibility is particularly important. The qualitative error analysis provides valuable insights for future research on improving LLM capabilities.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and research questions.
    *   Well-defined methodology and evaluation metrics.
    *   Use of diverse datasets to assess performance across different domains.
    *   Qualitative analysis providing insights into failure modes.
    *   The emphasis on *fidelity* versus *cohesion* is a crucial distinction.
    *   The observation that the performance characteristics are domain-dependent makes for an interesting finding.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The study uses only three commercial LLMs, limiting the generalizability of the findings. However, these *are* flagship models at the time of writing.
    *   The human evaluation is conducted by a single annotator, potentially introducing bias. Larger scale human evaluation is required, ideally with a diverse set of annotators to validate the findings. This is well-recognized in the limitations section.
    *   The dataset has likely been seen during training. The authors do address this, but this point does raise a question regarding how robust the findings are.
    *   The paper doesn't explore potential mitigation strategies or architectures to improve LLM performance on MSP.

*   **Impact:** The paper will likely influence future research by motivating the development of LLMs that can better model sentence-level coherence and long-range dependencies. The findings may also inform the design of more robust evaluation benchmarks for LLMs.  The clear demonstration of a practical limitation to current LLMs is valuable in the field.

*   **Justification for Score:** The paper offers a valuable contribution to the field by highlighting a practical limitation of current LLMs. The methodology is sound, and the findings have implications for the use of LLMs in various applications. While the single annotator limits the generalizability of the findings and some additional models would strengthen the study, the paper offers clear, well-reasoned insights that will influence ongoing research. The limitations are clearly described and recognized, showing transparency and good science.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Training-Free ANN-to-SNN Conversion for High-Performance Spiking Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07710v1)
- **Authors**: Jingya Wang, Xin Deng, Wenjie Wei, Dehao Zhang, Shuai Wang, Qian Sun, Jieyuan Zhang, Hanwen Liu, Ning Xie, Malu Zhang
- **Abstract**: Leveraging the event-driven paradigm, Spiking Neural Networks (SNNs) offer a promising approach for constructing energy-efficient Transformer architectures. Compared to directly trained Spiking Transformers, ANN-to-SNN conversion methods bypass the high training costs. However, existing methods still suffer from notable limitations, failing to effectively handle nonlinear operations in Transformer architectures and requiring additional fine-tuning processes for pre-trained ANNs. To address these issues, we propose a high-performance and training-free ANN-to-SNN conversion framework tailored for Transformer architectures. Specifically, we introduce a Multi-basis Exponential Decay (MBE) neuron, which employs an exponential decay strategy and multi-basis encoding method to efficiently approximate various nonlinear operations. It removes the requirement for weight modifications in pre-trained ANNs. Extensive experiments across diverse tasks (CV, NLU, NLG) and mainstream Transformer architectures (ViT, RoBERTa, GPT-2) demonstrate that our method achieves near-lossless conversion accuracy with significantly lower latency. This provides a promising pathway for the efficient and scalable deployment of Spiking Transformers in real-world applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

This paper presents a training-free ANN-to-SNN conversion method tailored for Transformer architectures.  The key contribution is the introduction of a Multi-Basis Exponential Decay (MBE) neuron, designed to more effectively approximate nonlinear operations (both single-variable and multi-variable) that are prevalent in Transformers.  The MBE neuron uses an exponential decay strategy and multi-basis encoding to avoid weight modifications in pre-trained ANNs.  The authors demonstrate near-lossless conversion accuracy and reduced latency compared to existing methods across various tasks (CV, NLU, NLG) and Transformer architectures (ViT, RoBERTa, GPT-2). The paper claims this approach offers a promising path for deploying Spiking Transformers in real-world applications.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The core novelty lies in the design of the MBE neuron and its application to Transformer conversion. The exponential decay strategy and multi-basis encoding are presented as a way to overcome limitations in previous FS neuron-based conversion attempts. Identifying and explicitly addressing the "excessive dependence on initialization" (EDI) and "global suboptimality" (GSO) problems of FS neurons is a valuable contribution. This is particularly crucial as prior methods often relied on modifications to pre-trained weights or imposed significant latency penalties. While FS neurons themselves are not new, the specific adaptation and extension of that concept through the MBE neuron within the Transformer architecture conversion context appears original. The overall training-free conversion and spike-driven multiplication contribute novelty.

*   **Significance:** SNNs hold promise for energy-efficient computation, making them appealing for resource-constrained devices.  Successfully converting powerful Transformer architectures to SNNs without retraining and with minimal performance degradation would be a significant advance. This paper demonstrates a notable reduction in conversion loss and latency compared to existing methods, suggesting it could accelerate the practical deployment of Spiking Transformers. However, the energy savings claims will need more detailed supporting data.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper effectively identifies limitations in existing approaches to Transformer A2S conversion, particularly concerning nonlinear operations and training overhead.
    *   **Technical Contribution:** The MBE neuron is well-motivated and theoretically analyzed. The paper provides error bounds for both FS neurons and the proposed MBE neuron. This theoretical rigor strengthens the validity of the approach.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experimental results are extensive, covering diverse tasks (CV, NLU, NLG) and multiple Transformer architectures. This provides strong evidence for the generalizability of the method.
    *   **Emphasis on Training-Free Conversion:** The training-free aspect is a significant advantage as it avoids the computational cost and potential issues associated with retraining pre-trained ANNs.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Empirical Energy Estimation:** The energy estimation relies on certain assumptions. More detailed energy measurements on actual hardware would provide a more convincing assessment of the method's energy efficiency.  The discussion of "greater energy savings" with specific operations could be expanded.
    *   **Complexity and Parameter Tuning:** While the paper stresses training-free conversion, details surrounding selection of parameters such as Tmax need further clarification. Parameter tuning, even without weight retraining, can require substantial effort.

*   **Potential Impact:**  The paper presents a practical approach for converting Transformer models to SNNs, making them potentially suitable for deployment on specialized hardware. If the approach can be successfully implemented on neuromorphic chips, it could have a significant impact on edge computing and other applications where energy efficiency is critical.

**Justification of Score:**

The paper demonstrates a good level of novelty and significance within the A2S-SNN field. The MBE neuron tackles a well-defined problem with an innovative design. However, the novelty might be considered incremental (adapting an FS neuron framework), and more extensive hardware validation is necessary for energy estimation claims. The analysis focuses on specific aspects of spike-based implementations for Transformer structures that can enable highly scalable model sizes.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### DoorDet: Semi-Automated Multi-Class Door Detection Dataset via Object Detection and Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07714v1)
- **Authors**: Licheng Zhang, Bach Le, Naveed Akhtar, Tuan Ngo
- **Abstract**: Accurate detection and classification of diverse door types in floor plans drawings is critical for multiple applications, such as building compliance checking, and indoor scene understanding. Despite their importance, publicly available datasets specifically designed for fine-grained multi-class door detection remain scarce. In this work, we present a semi-automated pipeline that leverages a state-of-the-art object detector and a large language model (LLM) to construct a multi-class door detection dataset with minimal manual effort. Doors are first detected as a unified category using a deep object detection model. Next, an LLM classifies each detected instance based on its visual and contextual features. Finally, a human-in-the-loop stage ensures high-quality labels and bounding boxes. Our method significantly reduces annotation cost while producing a dataset suitable for benchmarking neural models in floor plan analysis. This work demonstrates the potential of combining deep learning and multimodal reasoning for efficient dataset construction in complex real-world domains.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DoorDet, a new multi-class door detection dataset for architectural floor plans. The key contribution is a semi-automated pipeline for dataset construction. This pipeline combines a state-of-the-art object detector (Co-DETR) to detect doors and a large language model (LLM) to classify them into functional categories (e.g., bedroom door, emergency exit door). A human-in-the-loop refinement stage ensures high-quality labels. The paper presents dataset statistics and benchmarks several object detection models on DoorDet, demonstrating its utility for training and evaluation. The authors also perform ablation studies to assess the benefits of the LLM and human-in-the-loop refinement. The paper shows that the proposed method significantly reduces annotation cost while achieving reasonable accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using LLMs to classify architectural elements in floor plans is not entirely novel.  Other work has used LLMs for tasks like room type recognition or understanding design intent in CAD models. However, the *specific* application of using LLMs for *fine-grained multi-class door detection* within a semi-automated dataset construction pipeline *and the resulting dataset itself* is more novel. While existing datasets exist, the fine-grained, functionally-oriented labeling is a clear contribution. The emphasis on a human-in-the-loop approach is also valuable, as it acknowledges and addresses the limitations of purely automated methods. The introduction of metrics to correlate task difficulty and human intervention is also a positive contribution.

*   **Significance:** Accurate door detection is important for building compliance checking, accessibility analysis, and indoor scene understanding. The lack of publicly available datasets with fine-grained door type annotations hinders research in these areas. DoorDet fills this gap, providing a valuable resource for the community. The semi-automated pipeline also offers a practical approach to constructing datasets in other domains with limited resources. Demonstrating cross-domain adaptation is a strong point, indicating the real-world usability of models trained on DoorDet. The benchmarking and ablation studies provide valuable insights into the performance of different models and the impact of various components in the pipeline.
    The benchmarking is valuable for model comparisons. The discussion of class imbalance and its impact on model performance is also a strength.

*   **Weaknesses:** The LLM's dependency on clear visual cues and contextual information within the floor plans is a limitation. Also, the cost-effectiveness of using the LLM might depend on the specific API pricing and its availability.

*   **Impact and Future Influence:** The DoorDet dataset is likely to be adopted by researchers working on architectural floor plan analysis and object detection. The semi-automated pipeline could inspire similar approaches in other domains. It provides the community with a benchmark that will enable comparisons of various deep learning object detection methods.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of 7. The paper demonstrates sound methodology, provides a valuable contribution to the community with a new dataset, and offers insights into the performance of different object detection models and the benefits of using LLMs in the dataset construction process.
