### Generative Video Matting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07905v1)
- **Authors**: Yongtao Ge, Kangyang Xie, Guangkai Xu, Mingyu Liu, Li Ke, Longtao Huang, Hui Xue, Hao Chen, Chunhua Shen
- **Abstract**: Video matting has traditionally been limited by the lack of high-quality ground-truth data. Most existing video matting datasets provide only human-annotated imperfect alpha and foreground annotations, which must be composited to background images or videos during the training stage. Thus, the generalization capability of previous methods in real-world scenarios is typically poor. In this work, we propose to solve the problem from two perspectives. First, we emphasize the importance of large-scale pre-training by pursuing diverse synthetic and pseudo-labeled segmentation datasets. We also develop a scalable synthetic data generation pipeline that can render diverse human bodies and fine-grained hairs, yielding around 200 video clips with a 3-second duration for fine-tuning. Second, we introduce a novel video matting approach that can effectively leverage the rich priors from pre-trained video diffusion models. This architecture offers two key advantages. First, strong priors play a critical role in bridging the domain gap between synthetic and real-world scenes. Second, unlike most existing methods that process video matting frame-by-frame and use an independent decoder to aggregate temporal information, our model is inherently designed for video, ensuring strong temporal consistency. We provide a comprehensive quantitative evaluation across three benchmark datasets, demonstrating our approach's superior performance, and present comprehensive qualitative results in diverse real-world scenes, illustrating the strong generalization capability of our method. The code is available at https://github.com/aim-uofa/GVM.
- **Summary**: **Summary of "Generative Video Matting":** The paper addresses the challenges in video matting caused by limited availability of high-quality ground-truth data. Traditional datasets provide imperfect annotations, which hinder the generalization of video matting methods in real-world scenarios. The authors propose two main strategies: first, they underline the importance of large-scale pre-training with diverse synthetic and pseudo-labeled datasets. They introduce a scalable synthetic data generation pipeline that yields about 200 video clips featuring diverse human bodies and fine-grained hairs for fine-tuning. Second, they present a novel video matting approach that leverages pre-trained video diffusion models to enhance the inter-frame consistency and mitigate domain gaps. Their model offers enhanced temporal consistency and demonstrates superior performance in comprehensive evaluations against three benchmark datasets, illustrating strong generalization in various real-world contexts. The accompanying code is available for further exploration. **Critical Evaluation:** The paper exhibits significant novelty by addressing a prevalent issue in video matting: the reliance on imperfect ground-truth data, which limits the applicability of many existing methods. The dual approach—enhancing pre-training with synthetic data and utilizing diffusion models for temporal consistency—offers a progressive solution that diverges from conventional frame-by-frame processing. The introduction of a scalable synthetic data generation pipeline is particularly noteworthy, as it demonstrates a thorough understanding of the importance of diverse training data. Strengths: 1. **Innovative Approach**: The integration of generative modeling with video matting represents a meaningful advancement in the field. 2. **Temporal Consistency**: Addressing temporal coherence within video processing is a critical enhancement over earlier methods that often ignore temporal dynamics. 3. **Comprehensive Evaluation**: The empirical results showcase a rigorous assessment across benchmarks, which bolsters the claims of improved performance. Weaknesses: 1. **Synthetic Data Limitations**: While synthetic data generation is a strength, it can still pose challenges in terms of the realism and variability of scenarios compared to real-world data. 2. **Generalization Evidence**: Although the paper claims improved generalization, the real-world applicability may need further testing across a broader array of diverse datasets that reflect natural variations and occlusions. 3. **Computationally Intensive**: The reliance on large-scale pre-training and sophisticated models may require substantial computational resources, which may not be accessible to all researchers in the field. Overall, the paper contributes a noteworthy solution to a well-defined problem within video matting. Its innovative methodology and strong quantitative backing suggest it will encourage further research and development in video processing techniques. Therefore, the score awarded reflects the paper's substantial contribution and potential influence on future studies in the domain. **Score: 8**
- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### RSVLM-QA: A Benchmark Dataset for Remote Sensing Vision Language Model-based Question Answering
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07918v1)
- **Authors**: Xing Zi, Jinghao Xiao, Yunxiao Shi, Xian Tao, Jun Li, Ali Braytee, Mukesh Prasad
- **Abstract**: Visual Question Answering (VQA) in remote sensing (RS) is pivotal for interpreting Earth observation data. However, existing RS VQA datasets are constrained by limitations in annotation richness, question diversity, and the assessment of specific reasoning capabilities. This paper introduces RSVLM-QA dataset, a new large-scale, content-rich VQA dataset for the RS domain. RSVLM-QA is constructed by integrating data from several prominent RS segmentation and detection datasets: WHU, LoveDA, INRIA, and iSAID. We employ an innovative dual-track annotation generation pipeline. Firstly, we leverage Large Language Models (LLMs), specifically GPT-4.1, with meticulously designed prompts to automatically generate a suite of detailed annotations including image captions, spatial relations, and semantic tags, alongside complex caption-based VQA pairs. Secondly, to address the challenging task of object counting in RS imagery, we have developed a specialized automated process that extracts object counts directly from the original segmentation data; GPT-4.1 then formulates natural language answers from these counts, which are paired with preset question templates to create counting QA pairs. RSVLM-QA comprises 13,820 images and 162,373 VQA pairs, featuring extensive annotations and diverse question types. We provide a detailed statistical analysis of the dataset and a comparison with existing RS VQA benchmarks, highlighting the superior depth and breadth of RSVLM-QA's annotations. Furthermore, we conduct benchmark experiments on Six mainstream Vision Language Models (VLMs), demonstrating that RSVLM-QA effectively evaluates and challenges the understanding and reasoning abilities of current VLMs in the RS domain. We believe RSVLM-QA will serve as a pivotal resource for the RS VQA and VLM research communities, poised to catalyze advancements in the field.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces RSVLM-QA, a new large-scale benchmark dataset for Remote Sensing Vision Language Model-based Question Answering (RSVQA).  RSVLM-QA integrates data from existing remote sensing datasets (WHU, LoveDA, INRIA, iSAID) and uses a novel dual-track annotation generation pipeline powered by GPT-4.1. One track generates complex description-based questions, while the other automatically generates ground-truth-based counting questions. The dataset comprises 13,820 images and 162,373 VQA pairs. The authors benchmark RSVLM-QA using six mainstream VLMs to demonstrate its effectiveness and challenging nature. The dataset, code, and benchmark results are made publicly available.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The primary novelty lies in the dataset itself and the methodology for creating it. Integrating multiple existing datasets is not inherently novel, but the specific combination of datasets, the dual-track annotation process using LLMs, and the focus on object counting questions provide a unique contribution. The use of GPT-4.1 for generating annotations is relatively novel, as it leverages the latest advances in LLMs for dataset creation in the remote sensing domain. The prompt engineering applied to GPT-4.1 adds another layer of novelty.

*   **Significance:**  The paper addresses a clear need in the remote sensing community: the lack of large-scale, richly annotated, and diverse RSVQA datasets. Existing datasets often suffer from limited annotation richness, question diversity, and a lack of focus on quantitative reasoning. RSVLM-QA aims to overcome these limitations. By providing a challenging benchmark, the paper can potentially drive advancements in VLM research specifically tailored for remote sensing applications. Applications benefiting from better RS VQA range from environmental monitoring, urban planning, and disaster response. The availability of the dataset and benchmarks will likely facilitate further research in this area.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Scale and diversity:**  The dataset is significantly larger and more diverse than existing RS VQA datasets.
    *   **Rich annotations:**  The dual-track annotation pipeline results in detailed descriptions, spatial relationships, semantic tags, and ground-truth object counts.
    *   **Quantitative focus:**  The explicit emphasis on object counting questions addresses a critical need in RS image analysis.
    *   **Comprehensive benchmarking:**  The paper provides a thorough evaluation of several state-of-the-art VLMs on the dataset.
    *   **Publicly available:** The dataset, code, and benchmark results are publicly available, promoting reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **LLM dependence:** The reliance on GPT-4.1 for annotation generation introduces potential biases and limitations associated with LLMs, such as potential inaccuracies (hallucinations) even though quality control attempts to minimize it. This could impact the reliability of the annotations.
    *   **Automatic Evaluation:** The use of GPT-4.1 for evaluating VQA accuracy, while practical, could be a point of contention. While GPT-4.1 is powerful, it's not a perfect substitute for human judgment, and its assessments may not perfectly align with human perception of correctness.
    *   **Limited Analysis of Failure Modes**: The paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of the failure modes of the benchmarked VLMs, particularly in the object counting task. Identifying specific types of errors and the reasons behind them would be valuable for future research.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to become a valuable resource for the remote sensing and vision-language communities. It can stimulate research in several areas, including:
    *   Development of new VLMs specifically designed for remote sensing applications.
    *   Improvement of object counting and spatial reasoning capabilities in VLMs.
    *   Exploration of different annotation techniques for RS imagery.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, RSVLM-QA represents a significant contribution to the field. It addresses a clear need for better RS VQA datasets and provides a valuable resource for future research. While there are some limitations associated with the use of LLMs for annotation and evaluation, the overall benefits outweigh the drawbacks. The dataset's scale, diversity, and focus on quantitative reasoning make it a valuable addition to the field. Although the underlying idea of combining datasets is not brand new, the particular method in which it is done is relatively novel.

### Score Augmentation for Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07926v1)
- **Authors**: Liang Hou, Yuan Gao, Boyuan Jiang, Xin Tao, Qi Yan, Renjie Liao, Pengfei Wan, Di Zhang, Kun Gai
- **Abstract**: Diffusion models have achieved remarkable success in generative modeling. However, this study confirms the existence of overfitting in diffusion model training, particularly in data-limited regimes. To address this challenge, we propose Score Augmentation (ScoreAug), a novel data augmentation framework specifically designed for diffusion models. Unlike conventional augmentation approaches that operate on clean data, ScoreAug applies transformations to noisy data, aligning with the inherent denoising mechanism of diffusion. Crucially, ScoreAug further requires the denoiser to predict the augmentation of the original target. This design establishes an equivariant learning objective, enabling the denoiser to learn scores across varied denoising spaces, thereby realizing what we term score augmentation. We also theoretically analyze the relationship between scores in different spaces under general transformations. In experiments, we extensively validate ScoreAug on multiple benchmarks including CIFAR-10, FFHQ, AFHQv2, and ImageNet, with results demonstrating significant performance improvements over baselines. Notably, ScoreAug effectively mitigates overfitting across diverse scenarios, such as varying data scales and model capacities, while exhibiting stable convergence properties. Another advantage of ScoreAug over standard data augmentation lies in its ability to circumvent data leakage issues under certain conditions. Furthermore, we show that ScoreAug can be synergistically combined with traditional data augmentation techniques to achieve additional performance gains.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Score Augmentation (ScoreAug), a novel data augmentation framework specifically designed to mitigate overfitting in diffusion models, particularly in data-limited scenarios. Unlike conventional data augmentation, ScoreAug applies transformations to noisy data during the diffusion training process, aligning with the denoising objective. It requires the denoiser to predict the augmentation applied to the original target, creating an equivariant learning objective. This allows the denoiser to learn scores across different transformation spaces, achieving "score augmentation." The authors provide theoretical analysis and extensive experimental validation across multiple benchmarks (CIFAR-10, FFHQ, AFHQv2, ImageNet), demonstrating improved performance and robustness compared to baselines. The method also shows synergistic compatibility with traditional data augmentation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of applying augmentations to noisy data in diffusion models, forcing the denoiser to learn the inverse transformation, is novel. Existing augmentation techniques are typically applied to clean data, ignoring the inherent denoising process within diffusion models. This adaptation is a significant departure from standard practices. The derivation of the augmented loss function and the analysis of score function relationships under transformations are also noteworthy contributions.

*   **Significance:** Overfitting in diffusion models is an important practical problem, especially when dealing with limited data. ScoreAug directly addresses this issue with a targeted approach. The empirical results convincingly demonstrate improved generalization, data efficiency, and resistance to overfitting across diverse datasets and model architectures. The ability to combine ScoreAug with traditional augmentation techniques offers further practical benefits. The work has potential impact, since it can improve training of diffusion models in a variety of limited data regimes which can benefit a variety of applications.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   The paper is well-written and clearly explains the methodology.
    *   The theoretical analysis provides a solid foundation for the proposed approach.
    *   The experimental validation is comprehensive, covering a wide range of datasets, models, and ablation studies.
    *   The method is relatively easy to implement, and the results show consistent improvements.
    *   ScoreAug effectively circumvents certain data leakage issues associated with standard augmentation.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The method's effectiveness might be less pronounced when the base model is underfitting or already performs near the data limit.
    *   The convergence rate might be slower compared to vanilla training, requiring careful hyperparameter tuning.
    *   The experiments, while thorough, primarily focus on image generation. Extending the validation to other domains (e.g., audio, text) could further strengthen the paper's generalizability claims.
    *   While the theoretical analysis is presented, further elaborating on the connection between score augmentation and regularization effects in the context of deep learning could be a beneficial addition.
    *   The impact to the diffusion model convergence can be improved.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of diffusion modeling by providing a practical and effective technique for mitigating overfitting. It could encourage further research into augmentation strategies tailored to the specifics of generative models. The idea of incorporating equivariance constraints in the training objective may also inspire new approaches in other areas of deep learning. It can potentially benefit applications of diffusion models where data is limited.

**Justification of Score:**

I assign a score of **7**. The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of diffusion modeling by introducing ScoreAug. ### \(X\)-evolve: Solution space evolution powered by large language models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07932v1)
- **Authors**: Yi Zhai, Zhiqiang Wei, Ruohan Li, Keyu Pan, Shuo Liu, Lu Zhang, Jianmin Ji, Wuyang Zhang, Yu Zhang, Yanyong Zhang
- **Abstract**: While combining large language models (LLMs) with evolutionary algorithms (EAs) shows promise for solving complex optimization problems, current approaches typically evolve individual solutions, often incurring high LLM call costs. We introduce \(X\)-evolve, a paradigm-shifting method that instead evolves solution spaces \(X\) (sets of individual solutions) - subsets of the overall search space \(S\). In \(X\)-evolve, LLMs generate tunable programs wherein certain code snippets, designated as parameters, define a tunable solution space. A score-based search algorithm then efficiently explores this parametrically defined space, guided by feedback from objective function scores. This strategy enables broader and more efficient exploration, which can potentially accelerate convergence at a much lower search cost, requiring up to two orders of magnitude fewer LLM calls than prior leading methods. We demonstrate \(X\)-evolve's efficacy across three distinct hard optimization problems. For the cap set problem, we discover a larger partial admissible set, establishing a new tighter asymptotic lower bound for the cap set constant (\(C \ge 2.2203\)). In information theory, we uncover a larger independent set for the 15-vertex cycle graph (\(\mathcal{C}_{15}^{\boxtimes 5}\), size 19,946), thereby raising the known lower bound on its Shannon capacity. Furthermore, for the NP-hard online bin packing problem, we generate heuristics that consistently outperform standard strategies across established benchmarks. By evolving solution spaces, our method considerably improves search effectiveness, making it possible to tackle high-dimensional problems that were previously computationally prohibitive.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces X-evolve, a novel paradigm for combining large language models (LLMs) and evolutionary algorithms (EAs) to solve complex optimization problems. Unlike existing LLM+EA approaches that evolve individual solutions, X-evolve evolves solution *spaces*, which are subsets of the overall search space. It achieves this by prompting LLMs to generate tunable programs, where code snippets are designated as parameters, defining a tunable solution space. A score-based search algorithm, X-search, then efficiently explores this space guided by objective function scores. This strategy significantly reduces LLM call costs compared to evolving individual solutions. The authors demonstrate X-evolve's effectiveness across three challenging problems: cap set, Shannon capacity of cycle graphs, and online bin packing. They achieve state-of-the-art or improved results in each case, including establishing a new lower bound for the cap set constant.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The core idea of evolving solution *spaces* rather than individual solutions is the main novelty. While FunSearch inspired generating *programs* to generate *solutions*, X-evolve goes further by generating tunable programs representing solution *spaces*.  The introduction of "tunable markers" and the subsequent X-search algorithm to explore these defined spaces are key innovations. The use of score-based feedback within these LLM-defined spaces is also a valuable contribution, providing an evaluation loop that is frequently lacking.  The idea of compacting the "tunable programs" and reusing the compacted representation as a scored reference implementation for future evolutions is a good idea that contributes to longer-term learning and efficiency.

* **Significance:** The most significant aspect is the potential for substantial cost reduction (two orders of magnitude fewer LLM calls).  This makes LLM+EA approaches more practical for a wider range of computationally intensive problems. The empirical results are compelling, showcasing improvements or matching existing state-of-the-art results on challenging, well-established problems. Specifically, the new lower bound for the cap set constant, even a small improvement, is significant within the field of additive combinatorics. Rediscovering and improving on known Shannon Capacity results is also a good validation of the method. The results for the NP-hard Online Bin Packing problem are also significant.
    *   **Weakness:**  The paper could benefit from a more rigorous analysis comparing its performance across various hyperparameter settings.  The choice of *Kstall*, *batch size* and related settings, and the adaptive halving scheme seem critical but are only superficially explored in the abalation studies. The specific impact of these parameters on search efficiency and the quality of the solutions should be analyzed in detail.
    *   **Weakness:**  While the authors provide the X-evolve programs,  deeper insights into how the LLM is arriving at them might increase confidence in and understanding of the proposed method. The interpretability argument is good, but could go further.

* **Impact:** If the claim of significantly reduced LLM call costs holds up under broader scrutiny, X-evolve could have a considerable impact on the field. It makes LLM+EA approaches more accessible and encourages exploration of previously intractable problems.
    *   **Weakness:** The experiments are limited to just three domains.  Testing the method's generalizability across more diverse problem types would strengthen its claims of broader applicability.  Also, comparison to more LLM+EA methods might help to better understand how X-evolve stacks up against alternatives.

**Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to LLM+EA optimization. The introduction of solution space evolution and the associated X-search algorithm is a valuable contribution. The empirical results, especially the improved cap set lower bound, are compelling and demonstrate the method's effectiveness. The potential for significant cost reduction is a major strength. However, the paper could be improved by more detailed hyperparameter analysis and broader testing across diverse problem domains, further validation to confirm the generalizability of the improvements, as well as some more detailed exploration of how the LLMs are making their decisions (i.e., adding weight to the interpretabilty argument).

### Careful Queries, Credible Results: Teaching RAG Models Advanced Web Search Tools with Reinforcement Learning
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07956v1)
- **Authors**: Yuqin Dai, Shuo Yang, Guoqing Wang, Yong Deng, Zhanwei Zhang, Jun Yin, Pengyu Zeng, Zhenzhe Ying, Changhua Meng, Can Yi, Yuchen Zhou, Weiqiang Wang, Shuai Lu
- **Abstract**: Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) enhances large language models (LLMs) by integrating up-to-date external knowledge, yet real-world web environments present unique challenges. These limitations manifest as two key challenges: pervasive misinformation in the web environment, which introduces unreliable or misleading content that can degrade retrieval accuracy, and the underutilization of web tools, which, if effectively employed, could enhance query precision and help mitigate this noise, ultimately improving the retrieval results in RAG systems. To address these issues, we propose WebFilter, a novel RAG framework that generates source-restricted queries and filters out unreliable content. This approach combines a retrieval filtering mechanism with a behavior- and outcome-driven reward strategy, optimizing both query formulation and retrieval outcomes. Extensive experiments demonstrate that WebFilter improves answer quality and retrieval precision, outperforming existing RAG methods on both in-domain and out-of-domain benchmarks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces WebFilter, a novel Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) framework designed to improve answer accuracy and reliability in web-based environments. It addresses the challenges of misinformation and the underutilization of advanced web search tools. WebFilter formulates retrieval as a Markov Decision Process (MDP) and trains Large Language Models (LLMs) as information retrieval agents, equipped with advanced search operators and guided by a novel Information-Filtering Reward strategy. This strategy combines Source-restricting Reward (SR), which promotes the use of advanced search operators, with Retrieval-precision Reward (RR), which leverages external critique to assess retrieval quality. Experiments demonstrate that WebFilter achieves state-of-the-art QA performance across various in-domain and out-of-domain benchmarks, with a significant increase in the usage of advanced search operators.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates novelty in several aspects. Firstly, it explicitly addresses the problem of misinformation in web-based RAG environments. Secondly, it proposes a structured MDP formulation for retrieval, allowing the model to learn effective strategies for using advanced search operators. Finally, the Information-Filtering Reward strategy, combining behavioral (SR) and outcome-driven (RR) incentives, represents a novel approach to guiding LLMs toward precise and source-restricted retrieval. The focus on real-world web search with the added complexity of unreliable information distinguishes it from much prior RAG work focusing on static or trusted local data.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to improve the reliability and accuracy of RAG systems in real-world applications. By addressing the challenges of misinformation and tool underutilization, WebFilter offers a practical solution for building more robust and trustworthy RAG-based systems. The experimental results demonstrate substantial improvements in QA performance across diverse benchmarks, indicating the effectiveness of the proposed approach. The increased usage of advanced search operators suggests that WebFilter successfully guides the model toward more precise and targeted information retrieval. Furthermore, the out-of-domain generalization results suggest that WebFilter can be applied to a wide range of tasks.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem formulation and well-defined framework.
    *   Novel Information-Filtering Reward strategy.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation on diverse benchmarks.
    *   Demonstrated improvements in both answer quality and retrieval precision.
    *   Case studies provide insights into the model's intelligent retrieval behaviors.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The reliance on specific web search APIs (e.g., Google API) may limit the framework's portability to other search engines.
    *   The design of the Source-restricting Reward (SR) is rule-based, which may require manual tuning and adaptation to different search environments.
    *   The paper acknowledges that further improvements in reasoning abilities could enhance the framework's performance.
    *   The complexity of the training setup (requiring multiple LLMs and reinforcement learning) could make it challenging for some researchers to reproduce the results.

*   **Justification:**

The paper provides a tangible contribution to the field by proposing a novel RAG framework that addresses the challenges of misinformation and tool underutilization in web-based environments. The experimental results are compelling, demonstrating substantial improvements in QA performance and highlighting the effectiveness of the Information-Filtering Reward strategy. However, the reliance on specific web search APIs and the rule-based design of the SR strategy are limitations that could be addressed in future work. It is also true that the paper relies on fairly standard, if not slightly dated, pre-trained models in its experiments which limits its overall impact. Although improvements to RAG architectures are valuable, they also have a limited scope.

### Large Language Models for Subjective Language Understanding: A Survey
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07959v1)
- **Authors**: Changhao Song, Yazhou Zhang, Hui Gao, Ben Yao, Peng Zhang
- **Abstract**: Subjective language understanding refers to a broad set of natural language processing tasks where the goal is to interpret or generate content that conveys personal feelings, opinions, or figurative meanings rather than objective facts. With the advent of large language models (LLMs) such as ChatGPT, LLaMA, and others, there has been a paradigm shift in how we approach these inherently nuanced tasks. In this survey, we provide a comprehensive review of recent advances in applying LLMs to subjective language tasks, including sentiment analysis, emotion recognition, sarcasm detection, humor understanding, stance detection, metaphor interpretation, intent detection, and aesthetics assessment. We begin by clarifying the definition of subjective language from linguistic and cognitive perspectives, and we outline the unique challenges posed by subjective language (e.g. ambiguity, figurativeness, context dependence). We then survey the evolution of LLM architectures and techniques that particularly benefit subjectivity tasks, highlighting why LLMs are well-suited to model subtle human-like judgments. For each of the eight tasks, we summarize task definitions, key datasets, state-of-the-art LLM-based methods, and remaining challenges. We provide comparative insights, discussing commonalities and differences among tasks and how multi-task LLM approaches might yield unified models of subjectivity. Finally, we identify open issues such as data limitations, model bias, and ethical considerations, and suggest future research directions. We hope this survey will serve as a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners interested in the intersection of affective computing, figurative language processing, and large-scale language models.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a concise summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper abstract and partial OCR text.

**Summary:**

This paper presents a survey on the application of Large Language Models (LLMs) to Subjective Language Understanding (SLU) tasks. It defines SLU as encompassing various tasks related to interpreting or generating content that conveys personal feelings, opinions, or figurative meanings, such as sentiment analysis, emotion recognition, sarcasm detection, humor understanding, stance detection, metaphor recognition, intent detection, and aesthetics identification. The survey reviews recent advancements in using LLMs for these tasks, examining datasets, methods, and challenges. It emphasizes the significance of LLMs' capabilities in contextual understanding and reasoning for these nuanced tasks, while also acknowledging their limitations, such as potential biases and difficulties in handling complex linguistic phenomena. It also discusses challenges and potential directions for future research in this intersection of LLMs and SLU.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main novelty lies in providing a comprehensive overview of the rapidly evolving field of applying LLMs to SLU. While individual tasks like sentiment analysis and sarcasm detection have been studied extensively, this survey appears to be the *first* to synthesize recent advancements across the *entire* spectrum of subjective language tasks within the context of the LLM era. The unified framework proposed to cover these tasks is important. The critical assessment of how effective LLMs are in tasks previously considered challenging is valuable.

*   **Significance:** The survey is significant for several reasons:

    *   **Consolidation of Knowledge:** It synthesizes a large body of recent research (over 200 papers) into a coherent overview, making it easier for researchers and practitioners to grasp the current state of the field.
    *   **Identification of Trends and Challenges:** By examining the commonalities and differences across tasks, the survey identifies key trends (e.g., prompt engineering, multi-task learning) and challenges (e.g., bias, data limitations, handling context).
    *   **Guidance for Future Research:** The survey explicitly outlines open issues and suggests directions for future research, which can help to focus and accelerate progress in the field. The discussion of how to develop standard benchmarks to span the spectrum of subjective tasks is crucial.
    *   **Impact on Affective Computing and NLP:** The discussion of LLMs’ capabilities and limitations for tasks involving nuances in languages can help accelerate improvements in a wide variety of applications.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Broad Scope:** The survey covers a wide range of subjective language tasks, including topics that are often not covered in other surveys (e.g., metaphor recognition, aesthetics identification).
    *   **Emphasis on Understanding:** The survey focuses on *understanding* subjective language rather than just generating it, providing valuable insights into the cognitive aspects of these tasks.
    *   **Comprehensive Review:** The survey provides detailed descriptions of datasets, methods, and challenges for each task.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Timeliness:** Given the rapid pace of advancements in LLMs, the survey might become outdated relatively quickly. Regular updates or extensions would be necessary to maintain its relevance.  However, it is difficult to avoid this in a quickly evolving field.
    *   **Depth vs. Breadth:** While the survey covers a wide range of tasks, the depth of analysis for each task might be limited due to space constraints. More in-depth comparisons of specific methods or datasets could be beneficial. The full survey will contain more depth.

**Justification for Score:**

I assign a score of **8** to this paper. The paper provides a timely and valuable synthesis of a rapidly evolving field. The unified treatment of subjective language tasks within the LLM era, the identification of key trends and challenges, and the guidance for future research make it a significant contribution. While the paper might be limited by its timeliness and depth (likely unavoidable in a survey), its broad scope, emphasis on understanding, and comprehensive review of the literature outweigh these limitations.

### WeChat-YATT: A Simple, Scalable and Balanced RLHF Trainer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07970v1)
- **Authors**: Junyu Wu, Weiming Chang, Xiaotao Liu, Guanyou He, Tingfeng Xian, Haoqiang Hong, Boqi Chen, Haotao Tian, Tao Yang, Yunsheng Shi, Feng Lin, Ting Yao
- **Abstract**: Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF) has emerged as a prominent paradigm for training large language models and multimodal systems. Despite notable advances enabled by existing RLHF training frameworks, significant challenges remain in scaling to complex multimodal workflows and adapting to dynamic workloads. In particular, current systems often encounter limitations related to controller scalability when managing large models, as well as inefficiencies in orchestrating intricate RLHF pipelines, especially in scenarios that require dynamic sampling and resource allocation. In this paper, we introduce WeChat-YATT (Yet Another Transformer Trainer in WeChat), a simple, scalable, and balanced RLHF training framework specifically designed to address these challenges. WeChat-YATT features a parallel controller programming model that enables flexible and efficient orchestration of complex RLHF workflows, effectively mitigating the bottlenecks associated with centralized controller architectures and facilitating scalability in large-scale data scenarios. In addition, we propose a dynamic placement schema that adaptively partitions computational resources and schedules workloads, thereby significantly reducing hardware idle time and improving GPU utilization under variable training conditions. We evaluate WeChat-YATT across a range of experimental scenarios, demonstrating that it achieves substantial improvements in throughput compared to state-of-the-art RLHF training frameworks. Furthermore, WeChat-YATT has been successfully deployed to train models supporting WeChat product features for a large-scale user base, underscoring its effectiveness and robustness in real-world applications.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the WeChat-YATT paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces WeChat-YATT, a new Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF) training framework designed to address scalability, flexibility, and efficiency challenges in large-scale, multi-model training environments. WeChat-YATT's key innovations are a parallel controller programming model, which facilitates the orchestration of complex RLHF workflows, and a dynamic scaling placement schema that adaptively allocates resources to improve GPU utilization. The paper presents experimental results demonstrating throughput improvements compared to existing RLHF training frameworks. It also highlights the successful deployment of WeChat-YATT in training models that support WeChat product features, showcasing its real-world applicability and robustness.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The parallel controller programming model and dynamic placement schema are the core novel contributions. While hybrid parallelism and techniques like model swapping are not entirely new concepts, WeChat-YATT's specific implementation and integration of these elements to address RLHF bottlenecks present a significant advancement. The parallel controller architecture, in particular, offers a valuable alternative to centralized approaches, especially when dealing with large data and memory requirements. The dynamic placement strategy attempts to solve a known problem (resource allocation in dynamic workloads) with a standard ternary search algorithm. The value here might be in identifying this as a good fit rather than any algorithmic novelty.

*   **Significance:** The ability to scale RLHF training efficiently is crucial for developing advanced language models. WeChat-YATT's focus on optimizing hardware utilization and mitigating controller bottlenecks directly addresses key limitations of existing systems. The real-world deployment in WeChat suggests significant practical value. The presented experimental results offer compelling evidence of performance improvements. The open-sourcing of WeChat-YATT further enhances its significance, enabling other researchers and practitioners to build upon and adapt the framework.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear problem definition and well-motivated solutions.
    *   Detailed explanation of the design and implementation.
    *   Solid experimental evaluation demonstrating improved throughput.
    *   Real-world deployment showcasing practicality and robustness.
    *   Open-source release promoting further development and adoption.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The dynamic placement algorithm, while practical, is based on ternary search, a known, rather basic, optimization technique.
    *   The experimental evaluation could be more comprehensive. For example, comparing against more diverse RLHF frameworks (beyond VeRL) and examining performance under various hardware configurations would strengthen the results.
    *   While real-world deployment is mentioned, specific details regarding the production environment, the trained models, and the supported WeChat features are limited due to compliance constraints.

*   **Potential Influence:** WeChat-YATT has the potential to influence the development of future RLHF training frameworks. Its parallel controller programming model offers a viable alternative to existing controller architectures, while the dynamic scaling placement schema addresses the challenges of resource allocation in dynamic workloads. The framework's real-world success and open-source availability will likely encourage further research and development in this area.

*   **Rigorous Rationale:** The paper demonstrates that the core contributions of WeChat-YATT improve upon the existing state of the art in a quantifiable way, and the adoption within WeChat is a testament to the effectiveness of the tool. However, it lacks the algorithmic innovation that would make it a truly transformative paper. A higher score would require WeChat-YATT to offer something more innovative, or to have a significantly greater positive impact.

### Adaptive Multiple Access and Service Placement for Generative Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07978v1)
- **Authors**: Hamidreza Mazandarani, Mohammad Farhoudi, Masoud Shokrnezhad, Tarik Taleb
- **Abstract**: Generative Diffusion Models (GDMs) have emerged as key components of Generative Artificial Intelligence (GenAI), offering unparalleled expressiveness and controllability for complex data generation tasks. However, their deployment in real-time and mobile environments remains challenging due to the iterative and resource-intensive nature of the inference process. Addressing these challenges, this paper introduces a unified optimization framework that jointly tackles service placement and multiple access control for GDMs in mobile edge networks. We propose LEARN-GDM, a Deep Reinforcement Learning-based algorithm that dynamically partitions denoising blocks across heterogeneous edge nodes, while accounting for latent transmission costs and enabling adaptive reduction of inference steps. Our approach integrates a greedy multiple access scheme with a Double and Dueling Deep Q-Learning (D3QL)-based service placement, allowing for scalable, adaptable, and resource-efficient operation under stringent quality of service requirements. Simulations demonstrate the superior performance of the proposed framework in terms of scalability and latency resilience compared to conventional monolithic and fixed chain-length placement strategies. This work advances the state of the art in edge-enabled GenAI by offering an adaptable solution for GDM services orchestration, paving the way for future extensions toward semantic networking and co-inference across distributed environments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Adaptive Multiple Access and Service Placement for Generative Diffusion Models" addresses the challenges of deploying Generative Diffusion Models (GDMs) in mobile edge networks. It proposes LEARN-GDM, a Deep Reinforcement Learning (DRL) based algorithm, to jointly optimize service placement and multiple access control. LEARN-GDM dynamically partitions denoising blocks across edge nodes, accounts for transmission costs, and adapts the number of inference steps to balance performance and resource consumption. The algorithm integrates a greedy multiple access scheme with a Double and Dueling Deep Q-Learning (D3QL) approach for service placement. Simulations demonstrate its superiority in scalability and latency resilience compared to monolithic and fixed chain-length placement strategies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its *integrated approach* to addressing the challenges of deploying GDMs in mobile edge networks.  While individual components (DRL-based service placement, greedy MAC, edge computing for GDMs) have been explored separately, their simultaneous optimization within a unified framework specific to GDMs is a valuable contribution. The dynamic partitioning of denoising blocks based on available resources and user mobility also adds to the novelty. The authors build upon previous work by addressing limitations like the dynamic mobile nature of users which is very critical in mobile edge networks.
*   **Significance:**  GDMs are computationally intensive, and efficiently deploying them in resource-constrained mobile edge networks is a significant challenge. This paper's approach has the potential to enable real-time GDM applications in mobile environments, enhancing user experience in various domains such as autonomous driving, augmented reality, and content creation. The use of DRL makes the framework adaptable to dynamic network conditions and user behavior.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Problem Formulation:** The paper presents a well-defined optimization problem that considers crucial factors like service placement costs, transmission costs, user mobility, and multiple access constraints.
    *   **Practical Algorithm Design:** The LEARN-GDM algorithm is designed with practical considerations in mind, such as the use of a greedy MAC scheme for efficient channel allocation. The D3QL based service placement makes the system adaptive to dynamic network conditions.
    *   **Simulation Results:** The simulation results demonstrate the clear advantages of LEARN-GDM over several benchmark approaches, particularly in terms of scalability and latency resilience.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Complexity of DRL Training:** Training DRL agents can be computationally expensive and require careful tuning of hyperparameters. While the paper highlights the advantages of D3QL, it doesn't delve into the practical challenges of training and deploying such an agent in a real-world mobile edge network.
    *   **Simplifying Assumptions:** Several simplifying assumptions are made, such as the perfectly time-slotted communication channels and the limited mobility model. Real-world scenarios are much more complex, with asynchronous transmissions, interference, and more sophisticated mobility patterns.
    *   **Lack of Real-World Validation:** The paper relies solely on simulations. Validating the approach through real-world experiments or emulations would significantly strengthen the claims.
*   **Potential Impact:** The paper can influence research in edge-enabled AI, particularly concerning the orchestration of computationally intensive AI services in dynamic mobile environments. The unified optimization framework and the LEARN-GDM algorithm provide a solid foundation for future research and development in this area.
*   **Critical Point:** While DRL adapts well, it must be tested and trained using data close to what the agent is going to encounter. The simulations need to be more diverse.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution by addressing the challenging problem of deploying GDMs in mobile edge networks through a unified optimization framework and a DRL-based algorithm. The strengths lie in its comprehensive problem formulation, practical algorithm design, and simulation results demonstrating superior performance. However, the paper's weaknesses include the complexity of DRL training, simplifying assumptions, and lack of real-world validation. Because the simulation lacks some diversity, it is hard to claim scalability for real-world scenarios. Overall, the paper provides a valuable contribution to the field, albeit with some limitations, and its potential impact on future research is evident, therefore it has a good score.

### The Escalator Problem: Identifying Implicit Motion Blindness in AI for Accessibility
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07989v1)
- **Authors**: Xiantao Zhang
- **Abstract**: Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) hold immense promise as assistive technologies for the blind and visually impaired (BVI) community. However, we identify a critical failure mode that undermines their trustworthiness in real-world applications. We introduce the Escalator Problem -- the inability of state-of-the-art models to perceive an escalator's direction of travel -- as a canonical example of a deeper limitation we term Implicit Motion Blindness. This blindness stems from the dominant frame-sampling paradigm in video understanding, which, by treating videos as discrete sequences of static images, fundamentally struggles to perceive continuous, low-signal motion. As a position paper, our contribution is not a new model but rather to: (I) formally articulate this blind spot, (II) analyze its implications for user trust, and (III) issue a call to action. We advocate for a paradigm shift from purely semantic recognition towards robust physical perception and urge the development of new, human-centered benchmarks that prioritize safety, reliability, and the genuine needs of users in dynamic environments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "The Escalator Problem: Identifying Implicit Motion Blindness in AI for Accessibility" identifies a critical failure mode in current Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) regarding their ability to perceive motion in videos.  The authors term this "Implicit Motion Blindness" and use the inability of MLLMs to determine the direction of an escalator as a canonical example. They argue that this stems from the dominant frame-sampling paradigm used in video understanding, which treats videos as discrete sequences of static images, hindering the perception of continuous, low-signal motion.  The paper is a position paper, not proposing a new model but rather articulating the problem, analyzing its implications for user trust in assistive technologies for the Blind and Visually Impaired (BVI) community, and issuing a call to action for the research community. The authors advocate for a shift towards robust physical perception and the development of new, human-centered benchmarks that prioritize safety and reliability in dynamic environments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Novelty:** The core novelty lies in *identifying and formally articulating* the "Implicit Motion Blindness" problem within the specific context of AI for accessibility. While the frame-sampling limitations in video understanding are known, explicitly linking it to a failure mode in assistive technologies and highlighting the implications for user trust is a valuable contribution. The use of the "Escalator Problem" as a simple, relatable, and illustrative example is also effective. However, there's no novel algorithm or architecture proposed. The paper points towards future directions.

**Significance:** The paper's significance stems from its potential to re-direct the research focus within the AI for accessibility field.  Current benchmarks often reward semantic recognition while neglecting the importance of physical perception, which this paper effectively challenges.  The emphasis on user trust and the need for human-centered benchmarks is crucial for the real-world deployment of these technologies. If the community takes this paper seriously, it could lead to the development of more robust and reliable assistive tools. The discussion on the ethical implication, how untrustworthy AI systems can introduce cognitive load to vulnerable users, is very insightful.

**Strengths:**

*   **Clear Articulation of the Problem:** The paper clearly defines and illustrates the "Implicit Motion Blindness" problem with compelling examples.
*   **Focus on User Trust:** Highlighting the importance of user trust in assistive technologies is a crucial and often overlooked aspect.
*   **Call to Action:** The paper provides a clear and actionable call to action for the research community, suggesting specific avenues for exploration.
*   **Well-written and Accessible:**  The paper is well-written and accessible, making it easy for a broad audience to understand the key issues.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **No Concrete Solutions:** The paper is primarily a problem statement and doesn't offer specific solutions or algorithms to address the identified issues. However, the discussion on hybrid approaches is a good direction to take.
*   **Limited Empirical Evaluation:** While the paper demonstrates the failure of MLLMs on the Escalator Problem, a more comprehensive empirical evaluation across a wider range of motion-related tasks could strengthen the argument.
*   **Overstates the 'Crisis'**: Calling it a 'crisis of trust' might be an overstatement. This depends on the user's prior experience with these AI systems and their understanding of AI limitations.

**Potential Influence:**

This paper has the potential to influence the AI for accessibility research community by:

*   Raising awareness about the limitations of current MLLMs in perceiving motion.
*   Encouraging the development of new benchmarks that prioritize safety, reliability, and user trust.
*   Inspiring research into new architectures and algorithms that are better suited for processing dynamic visual information.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a valuable and well-articulated critique of current approaches to AI for accessibility. Its novelty lies in the specific framing of the "Implicit Motion Blindness" problem and its emphasis on user trust. However, the lack of concrete solutions and limited empirical evaluation prevents it from being a truly groundbreaking contribution. The high potential for impact on the AI for accessibility research justifies a higher rating.

### WideSearch: Benchmarking Agentic Broad Info-Seeking
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.07999v1)
- **Authors**: Ryan Wong, Jiawei Wang, Junjie Zhao, Li Chen, Yan Gao, Long Zhang, Xuan Zhou, Zuo Wang, Kai Xiang, Ge Zhang, Wenhao Huang, Yang Wang, Ke Wang
- **Abstract**: From professional research to everyday planning, many tasks are bottlenecked by wide-scale information seeking, which is more repetitive than cognitively complex. With the rapid development of Large Language Models (LLMs), automated search agents powered by LLMs offer a promising solution to liberate humans from this tedious work. However, the capability of these agents to perform such "wide-context" collection reliably and completely remains largely unevaluated due to a lack of suitable benchmarks. To bridge this gap, we introduce WideSearch, a new benchmark engineered to evaluate agent reliability on these large-scale collection tasks. The benchmark features 200 manually curated questions (100 in English, 100 in Chinese) from over 15 diverse domains, grounded in real user queries. Each task requires agents to collect large-scale atomic information, which could be verified one by one objectively, and arrange it into a well-organized output. A rigorous five-stage quality control pipeline ensures the difficulty, completeness, and verifiability of the dataset. We benchmark over 10 state-of-the-art agentic search systems, including single-agent, multi-agent frameworks, and end-to-end commercial systems. Most systems achieve overall success rates near 0\%, with the best performer reaching just 5\%. However, given sufficient time, cross-validation by multiple human testers can achieve a near 100\% success rate. These results demonstrate that present search agents have critical deficiencies in large-scale information seeking, underscoring urgent areas for future research and development in agentic search. Our dataset, evaluation pipeline, and benchmark results have been publicly released at https://widesearch-seed.github.io/
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the "WideSearch: Benchmarking Agentic Broad Info-Seeking" paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces WideSearch, a new benchmark specifically designed to evaluate the capabilities of LLM-powered search agents in performing broad, large-scale information-seeking tasks. The benchmark consists of 200 manually curated questions (100 in English and 100 in Chinese) across 15 diverse domains, mirroring real-world user queries. Tasks require agents to collect comprehensive, verifiable information and organize it into a structured output (tables). The authors rigorously curate the dataset, benchmark over 10 state-of-the-art agentic search systems, and find that most systems achieve near-zero success rates, highlighting critical deficiencies in current agentic search capabilities. The paper also demonstrates that even humans struggle with such wide-scale search tasks, suggesting the inherent difficulty of the benchmark and potential solutions with multi-agent systems.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**
    *   **Strength:** The paper introduces a genuinely novel benchmark, WideSearch, which fills a crucial gap in evaluating agentic information seeking. Unlike existing benchmarks focusing on deep reasoning or report generation, WideSearch targets the ability of agents to efficiently and reliably gather broad, verifiable information across multiple entities. This "wide-context" evaluation is a valuable and novel contribution.
    *   **Weakness:** While the benchmark itself is novel, the underlying techniques used by the evaluated agents (LLMs, search engine APIs, webpage scraping) are not new. The paper focuses on evaluating existing technologies in a new context rather than presenting groundbreaking algorithmic advancements.

*   **Significance:**
    *   **Strength:** The paper's significance lies in its ability to reveal the current limitations of state-of-the-art agentic search systems. The near-zero success rates underscore the fact that agents are far from being reliable in real-world scenarios involving large-scale information seeking. This identification of critical deficiencies can stimulate further research and development in areas such as task planning, iterative refinement, and evidence utilization. The paper's error analysis provides a valuable roadmap for future research.
    *   **Strength:** The rigorous methodology used in creating the WideSearch benchmark adds to the significance of the paper. The multi-stage curation process, hybrid evaluation system, and consistency analysis with human judgment ensure that the benchmark is reliable and accurately reflects real-world challenges.
    *   **Weakness:** The evaluation is limited to readily available commercial tools and relatively simple agent architectures. While useful for establishing a baseline, the conclusions might not fully generalize to highly specialized or proprietary systems. Furthermore, the paper uses mostly "out-of-the-box" LLM setups with minimal prompt engineering in its modular agent setups. Advanced prompt optimization techniques or custom LLM training might significantly improve performance.

*   **Impact:**
    *   **Potential Influence:** The WideSearch benchmark is likely to become a standard tool for evaluating and comparing agentic search systems. It provides a valuable framework for researchers and developers to assess progress and identify areas for improvement. The benchmark's emphasis on reliability and completeness could shift the focus of agentic search research toward more practical, real-world applications.
    *   **Weakness**: The paper could benefit from a more in-depth exploration of potential solutions to the identified challenges. While the authors suggest multi-agent systems, a more detailed discussion of specific architectures or algorithms would enhance the paper's impact.

*   **Score Justification:**

The paper presents a novel and rigorously created benchmark, WideSearch, that exposes critical deficiencies in current agentic search systems. This identification of limitations and the roadmap for future research make a significant contribution to the field. However, the paper does not introduce any new algorithmic techniques and primarily evaluates existing technologies. Considering the significant contribution to the field balanced by the relative lack of algorithmic novelty, I assign a score of:

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Progressive Depth Up-scaling via Optimal Transport
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08011v1)
- **Authors**: Mingzi Cao, Xi Wang, Nikolaos Aletras
- **Abstract**: Scaling Large Language Models (LLMs) yields performance gains but incurs substantial training costs. Depth up-scaling offers training efficiency by adding new layers to pre-trained models. However, most existing methods copy or average weights from base layers, neglecting neuron permutation differences. This limitation can potentially cause misalignment that harms performance. Inspired by applying Optimal Transport (OT) for neuron alignment, we propose Optimal Transport Depth Up-Scaling (OpT-DeUS). OpT-DeUS aligns and fuses Transformer blocks in adjacent base layers via OT for new layer creation, to mitigate neuron permutation mismatch between layers. OpT-DeUS achieves better overall performance and offers improved training efficiency than existing methods for continual pre-training and supervised fine-tuning across different model sizes. To further evaluate the impact of interpolation positions, our extensive analysis shows that inserting new layers closer to the top results in higher training efficiency due to shorter back-propagation time while obtaining additional performance gains.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces OpT-DeUS (Optimal Transport Depth Up-Scaling), a novel approach for progressively expanding the depth of pre-trained large language models (LLMs).  The core idea is to use optimal transport (OT) to align and fuse adjacent Transformer blocks in the base model when creating new layers. This is intended to mitigate neuron permutation mismatch, a problem where functionally similar neurons in different layers are not indexed the same, which can harm performance when using simple copying or averaging for initialization. The method inserts the new layers in the top half of the base model and sets certain block weights to zero. Experiments demonstrate that OpT-DeUS outperforms existing depth up-scaling baselines in both continual pre-training and supervised fine-tuning across different model sizes. Furthermore, the paper analyzes the impact of the insertion positions of the new layers and finds that inserting them closer to the top of the network results in better training efficiency due to shorter backpropagation times.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:**  The paper's core novelty lies in the use of optimal transport for aligning and fusing layers during depth up-scaling, specifically addressing the neuron permutation mismatch problem.  While OT has been used for model merging, applying it within the context of depth expansion and progressive training is relatively novel. The comprehensive study of interpolation positions, demonstrating the benefits of inserting layers in the upper portion of the network, also adds to the contribution.
* **Significance:** Addressing training efficiency for LLMs is a significant challenge.  Depth up-scaling is a promising technique, and the paper provides a valuable improvement over existing methods that rely on simpler initialization strategies. The performance gains over baselines across different model sizes suggest the method's robustness and scalability. The findings regarding the optimal insertion point contribute practically to efficient training. The analysis of the contribution of OT by comparing OpT-DeUS and Avg-DeUS strengthens the importance of the approach.
* **Strengths:**
    * **Clear problem definition:**  The paper clearly articulates the issue of neuron permutation mismatch and its potential impact on depth up-scaling.
    * **Well-motivated approach:** The use of OT is well-motivated by the need for neuron alignment.
    * **Strong experimental results:**  OpT-DeUS consistently outperforms baselines across various experimental settings, including different model sizes and training stages.
    * **Comprehensive analysis:** The paper includes a thorough analysis of interpolation positions and their impact on training efficiency.
    * **Robustness to model size:** The benefits of OpT-DeUS are demonstrated on both smaller (1.72B) and larger (11.5B) models.
* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Computational overhead of OT:** While the paper shows overall training efficiency, the OT calculation itself can be computationally expensive. The paper could provide a more detailed analysis of the computational cost and trade-offs of using OT compared to simpler initialization methods.
    * **Limited exploration of OT variants:** The paper primarily uses a specific implementation of OT (Sinkhorn-Knopp). Exploring different OT algorithms or cost functions might yield further improvements.
    * **Evaluation on limited datasets**: The tasks used in the evaluation are primarily "knowledge-related tasks". Further evaluations on tasks assessing reasoning or text generation (e.g., open-ended generation) would provide a more thorough view of its effectiveness.
    * **Hyperparameter sensitivity**: Though the authors have discussed the regularization parameter of Sinkhorn-Knopp algorithm (0.06), there may be other hyperparameters that can significantly impact the performance. It would enhance the credibility of the results by addressing the hyperparameter sensitivity of the method.
* **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to influence the development of more efficient and effective depth up-scaling techniques for LLMs. The findings on neuron alignment and interpolation positions provide valuable insights for future research. It demonstrates a practical approach to improve training efficiency while maintaining performance on downstream tasks.

**Justification for the Score:**

Considering the novelty, significance, strengths, and weaknesses, I would assign a score of **7**.

The paper makes a novel contribution by applying OT to progressive depth up-scaling. This addresses a specific problem (neuron permutation mismatch) and provides tangible improvements over existing methods. The experiments are well-designed and the analysis is comprehensive. However, the computational overhead of OT and the potential for further optimization through different OT variants or hyperparameter tuning leave room for future work.  The use of knowledge-related tasks is a good starting point, and future work may focus on applying the approach on tasks concerning reasoning or text generation, as well.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### EchoAid: Enhancing Livestream Shopping Accessibility for the DHH Community
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08020v1)
- **Authors**: Zeyu Yang, Zheng Wei, Yang Zhang, Xian Xu, Changyang He, Muzhi Zhou, Pan Hui
- **Abstract**: Livestream shopping platforms often overlook the accessibility needs of the Deaf and Hard of Hearing (DHH) community, leading to barriers such as information inaccessibility and overload. To tackle these challenges, we developed \textit{EchoAid}, a mobile app designed to improve the livestream shopping experience for DHH users. \textit{EchoAid} utilizes advanced speech-to-text conversion, Rapid Serial Visual Presentation (RSVP) technology, and Large Language Models (LLMs) to simplify the complex information flow in live sales environments. We conducted exploratory studies with eight DHH individuals to identify design needs and iteratively developed the \textit{EchoAid} prototype based on feedback from three participants. We then evaluate the performance of this system in a user study workshop involving 38 DHH participants. Our findings demonstrate the successful design and validation process of \textit{EchoAid}, highlighting its potential to enhance product information extraction, leading to reduced cognitive overload and more engaging and customized shopping experiences for DHH users.
- **Summary**: Here's a concise summary and a rigorous evaluation of the paper, including a novelty/significance score:

**Summary:**

The paper "EchoAid: Enhancing Livestream Shopping Accessibility for the DHH Community" presents a mobile application, EchoAid, designed to improve the livestream shopping experience for Deaf and Hard of Hearing (DHH) users.  EchoAid addresses information inaccessibility and overload by using speech-to-text conversion, Rapid Serial Visual Presentation (RSVP) technology, and Large Language Models (LLMs) to simplify information flow. The authors conducted exploratory studies to identify design needs, developed an iterative prototype, and evaluated the system in a user study with 38 DHH participants.  The results suggest that EchoAid successfully enhances product information extraction, reduces cognitive overload, and creates a more engaging and customized shopping experience.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Addresses a Specific, Underserved Need:** The paper tackles a very real accessibility gap in the increasingly popular domain of livestream shopping. The focus on the DHH community is well-motivated.
    *   **Comprehensive Design and Evaluation:**  The paper follows a solid research methodology, from initial needs assessment to prototype development and user evaluation. The iterative design process based on user feedback is a strength.
    *   **Well-chosen technologies:** RSVP technology coupled with Large Language Models (LLMs) is a good approach for addressing the core issues of information overload and accessibility for DHH community.
    *   **Quantitative results:** The authors collected quantitative results and statistical analyses which helps highlight the effectiveness of their system.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Incremental Novelty:** While the application is well-executed, the individual technologies used (speech-to-text, RSVP, LLMs) are not novel in themselves.  The novelty lies primarily in the *combination* of these techniques in the specific context of livestream shopping.
    *   **Limited Generalizability:** The user study focuses on a specific demographic and cultural context (DHH community in China). It's not clear how well the findings generalize to other populations or livestreaming platforms. Also, all the users were platform users, they could have inherent bias on the research.
    *   **Lack of Comparative Evaluation:** While the paper compares EchoAid to iFlytek, a broader comparison to other accessibility solutions or techniques (e.g., sign language interpretation) would strengthen the evaluation.
    *   **Limited Discussion of Limitations:** While the paper acknowledges some limitations in the discussion section, the authors did not include those limitations in the main sections of their paper.

*   **Significance:**
    *   **Practical Contribution:** The EchoAid application has the potential to directly benefit the DHH community by making livestream shopping more accessible and enjoyable. The paper also provides a blueprint for developing similar accessibility tools in other domains.
    *   **Empirical Evidence:**  The user study provides empirical evidence that the combination of technologies can effectively reduce cognitive load and improve information retention for DHH users in a complex environment.
    *   **Stimulates Further Research:** The paper highlights the need for more research on accessibility in emerging online environments and encourages the development of more inclusive technologies.

**Justification of Score:**

I'm assigning a score of **7**.  While the paper doesn't present groundbreaking technological innovations, its significance stems from:

*   Its strong focus on an underserved population within a booming e-commerce sector.
*   The thoughtful integration of existing technologies to solve a specific accessibility problem.
*   The rigorous user-centered design and evaluation process, providing empirical evidence of the system's effectiveness.

The limitations related to novelty and generalizability prevent a higher score. A more thorough comparative evaluation and a broader discussion of the paper's limitations would have strengthened the paper's contribution.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.HC
- **Score**: 7/10

### Robust Anomaly Detection in O-RAN: Leveraging LLMs against Data Manipulation Attacks
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08029v1)
- **Authors**: Thusitha Dayaratne, Ngoc Duy Pham, Viet Vo, Shangqi Lai, Sharif Abuadbba, Hajime Suzuki, Xingliang Yuan, Carsten Rudolph
- **Abstract**: The introduction of 5G and the Open Radio Access Network (O-RAN) architecture has enabled more flexible and intelligent network deployments. However, the increased complexity and openness of these architectures also introduce novel security challenges, such as data manipulation attacks on the semi-standardised Shared Data Layer (SDL) within the O-RAN platform through malicious xApps. In particular, malicious xApps can exploit this vulnerability by introducing subtle Unicode-wise alterations (hypoglyphs) into the data that are being used by traditional machine learning (ML)-based anomaly detection methods. These Unicode-wise manipulations can potentially bypass detection and cause failures in anomaly detection systems based on traditional ML, such as AutoEncoders, which are unable to process hypoglyphed data without crashing. We investigate the use of Large Language Models (LLMs) for anomaly detection within the O-RAN architecture to address this challenge. We demonstrate that LLM-based xApps maintain robust operational performance and are capable of processing manipulated messages without crashing. While initial detection accuracy requires further improvements, our results highlight the robustness of LLMs to adversarial attacks such as hypoglyphs in input data. There is potential to use their adaptability through prompt engineering to further improve the accuracy, although this requires further research. Additionally, we show that LLMs achieve low detection latency (under 0.07 seconds), making them suitable for Near-Real-Time (Near-RT) RIC deployments.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper addresses the vulnerability of Open Radio Access Network (O-RAN) architectures to data manipulation attacks, specifically targeting the Shared Data Layer (SDL) through malicious xApps. The core issue is that malicious xApps can introduce subtle Unicode-wise alterations (hypoglyphs) that bypass traditional machine learning (ML)-based anomaly detection systems.  The paper proposes and evaluates using Large Language Models (LLMs) for anomaly detection within the O-RAN architecture as a more robust solution. The results indicate that LLM-based xApps can process manipulated messages without crashing and maintain operational performance. While initial detection accuracy needs improvement, the paper demonstrates the LLM's resilience to adversarial attacks and shows that LLMs can achieve low detection latency, making them suitable for Near-Real-Time (Near-RT) RIC deployments. The paper highlights LLM robustness in contrast to the failure of traditional AutoEncoder ML models when faced with the same data.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:**  The novelty lies in specifically applying LLMs for anomaly detection in the O-RAN context, particularly focusing on the SDL vulnerability related to data manipulation attacks using hypoglyphs. While using LLMs for anomaly detection is not entirely new in general, the specific application to the O-RAN SDL with this attack vector contributes to the field. Prior works have explored ML-based detection in O-RAN, but the paper correctly identified the limitations of these methods in the face of even simple evasion tactics such as unicode character manipulations.

*   **Significance:** The significance comes from addressing a real and growing security concern in O-RAN deployments. The under-standardized SDL and the open nature of xApps create a significant attack surface. The paper provides evidence of vulnerability using a realistic attack (hypoglyphs), which is more subtle than simply deleting data. The demonstration of LLM's resilience to such attacks is valuable. LLM based approach shows its superiority against traditional approaches that are easily bypassed by simple data manipulations. The results highlighting low latency times contribute to the practicality of using LLMs for near-real-time security monitoring. The study points towards an important direction for robust security solutions for O-RAN architecture by leveraging modern language models.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clearly defines the threat model and vulnerability.
    *   Demonstrates the vulnerability of traditional ML-based approaches with concrete examples.
    *   Provides empirical evidence for LLM resilience.
    *   Addresses a practical issue in a relevant and emerging architecture (O-RAN).
    *   Evaluates the latency of the LLM-based detection, showing its suitability for real-time deployment.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The F1 scores achieved by the LLM-based system are relatively low, indicating room for significant improvement in detection accuracy. The paper correctly identifies the need for "further optimization and prompt engineering."  This is a major point that makes the novelty more about the potential of the approach than a realized solution.
    *   The dataset used is relatively small.
    *   The evaluation could benefit from comparison with other anomaly detection techniques (beyond the failing AutoEncoder model).
    *   Limited investigation on the prompt engineering to improve performance.
    *   Lack of ablation studies on the different components of the LLM.
    *   Cost/resource considerations when deploying the LLM in operational setting.
    *   No investigation done on the impact of advanced data manipulation (adversarial attacks) on LLM detection capabilities.
