- **Abstract**: Spoken Language Models (SLMs), which extend Large Language Models (LLMs) to perceive speech inputs, have gained increasing attention for their potential to advance speech understanding tasks. However, despite recent progress, studies show that SLMs often struggle to generalize across datasets, even for trained languages and tasks, raising concerns about whether they process speech in a text-like manner as intended. A key challenge underlying this limitation is the modality gap between speech and text representations. The high variability in speech embeddings may allow SLMs to achieve strong in-domain performance by exploiting unintended speech variations, ultimately hindering generalization. To mitigate this modality gap, we introduce Optimal Transport Regularization (OTReg), a method that formulates speech-text alignment as an optimal transport problem and derives a regularization loss to improve SLM training. In each training iteration, OTReg first establishes a structured correspondence between speech and transcript embeddings by determining the optimal transport plan, then incorporates the regularization loss based on this transport plan to optimize SLMs in generating speech embeddings that align more effectively with transcript embeddings. OTReg is lightweight, requiring no additional labels or learnable parameters, and integrates seamlessly into existing SLM training procedures. Extensive multilingual ASR experiments demonstrate that OTReg enhances speech-text alignment, mitigates the modality gap, and consequently improves SLM generalization across diverse datasets.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces Optimal Transport Regularization (OTReg) to improve speech-text alignment in Spoken Language Models (SLMs). OTReg formulates speech-text alignment as an optimal transport problem, deriving a regularization loss to encourage SLMs to generate speech embeddings that align better with transcript embeddings. The method is lightweight, requires no additional labels or learnable parameters, and integrates seamlessly into existing SLM training pipelines. Experimental results on multilingual ASR tasks demonstrate that OTReg enhances speech-text alignment, mitigates the modality gap, and improves SLM generalization across diverse datasets.  The paper also proposes an OT-based compression method to reduce redundancy in the speech embeddings.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its application of optimal transport to regularize speech-text alignment within SLMs and the development of a practical and efficient regularization loss. While optimal transport has been used for alignment in other speech processing tasks, this specific formulation for SLMs, combined with a sparsity constraint, is novel. The OT-based compression idea is interesting but may be less novel, as content-aware compression techniques already exist.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a critical challenge in SLMs: the modality gap between speech and text. By improving speech-text alignment, OTReg enhances the generalization capabilities of SLMs, making them more robust across different datasets and languages. This is significant because generalization is a major bottleneck for current SLM research. The experimental results support these claims, demonstrating improved performance on multilingual ASR tasks, especially in cross-domain scenarios. The lack of learnable parameters is also highly significant, making this method incredibly practical.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-explained methodology.
    *   Empirical validation with multiple datasets and languages.
    *   Lightweight and easy-to-integrate approach.
    *   Comprehensive ablation studies analyzing the influence of λот.
    * The visualization and comparison of baseline and OTReg on aligned embeddings is very helpful.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   The paper could benefit from a more extensive comparison with existing alignment techniques beyond just CTC, particularly other cross-modal alignment methods. More extensive benchmarks on more diverse tasks would also solidify the generalizability.
    *   The OT-based compression section seems somewhat decoupled from the main focus of OTReg. While it's a related component, its contribution isn't as clearly demonstrated or integrated into the core results.
    *   The computational cost of the Sinkhorn algorithm, even with regularization, might be a concern for very large SLMs or datasets. The paper doesn't explicitly address this or provide a thorough analysis of the runtime overhead.
    * Some of the description in the methodology is ambiguous and requires referencing external work to fully understand.
    * While the paper claims that OTReg helps to mitigate the need for large and diverse datasets, quantitative results are needed to support this claim.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the field of SLM research by providing a practical and effective method for improving speech-text alignment and generalization. The simplicity and ease of integration of OTReg make it likely to be adopted by other researchers and practitioners working on SLMs. However, its influence will depend on its performance on more diverse datasets and its ability to scale to larger models.

* **Rationale for the Score:** The paper presents a novel and practical approach to improve speech-text alignment in SLMs, addressing a critical challenge in the field. The experimental results demonstrate its effectiveness in enhancing generalization, and the method's lightweight nature makes it easy to adopt. While there are some weaknesses, such as the need for more extensive comparisons and limited discussion of computational costs, the paper's overall contribution is significant.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### FantasyStyle: Controllable Stylized Distillation for 3D Gaussian Splatting
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08136v1)
- **Authors**: Yitong Yang, Yinglin Wang, Changshuo Wang, Huajie Wang, Shuting He
- **Abstract**: The success of 3DGS in generative and editing applications has sparked growing interest in 3DGS-based style transfer. However, current methods still face two major challenges: (1) multi-view inconsistency often leads to style conflicts, resulting in appearance smoothing and distortion; and (2) heavy reliance on VGG features, which struggle to disentangle style and content from style images, often causing content leakage and excessive stylization. To tackle these issues, we introduce \textbf{FantasyStyle}, a 3DGS-based style transfer framework, and the first to rely entirely on diffusion model distillation. It comprises two key components: (1) \textbf{Multi-View Frequency Consistency}. We enhance cross-view consistency by applying a 3D filter to multi-view noisy latent, selectively reducing low-frequency components to mitigate stylized prior conflicts. (2) \textbf{Controllable Stylized Distillation}. To suppress content leakage from style images, we introduce negative guidance to exclude undesired content. In addition, we identify the limitations of Score Distillation Sampling and Delta Denoising Score in 3D style transfer and remove the reconstruction term accordingly. Building on these insights, we propose a controllable stylized distillation that leverages negative guidance to more effectively optimize the 3D Gaussians. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method consistently outperforms state-of-the-art approaches, achieving higher stylization quality and visual realism across various scenes and styles.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "FantasyStyle: Controllable Stylized Distillation for 3D Gaussian Splatting" presents a novel approach to style transfer for 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) scenes. It addresses two key challenges in existing methods: multi-view inconsistency and content leakage from style images.  The proposed method, FantasyStyle, relies entirely on diffusion model distillation, incorporating two core components: (1) Multi-View Frequency Consistency (MVFC) to enhance cross-view consistency by selectively reducing low-frequency components in the latent space, and (2) Controllable Stylized Distillation (CSD), which uses negative guidance to suppress content leakage from the style image and remove reconstruction term from the objective.  The authors demonstrate through qualitative and quantitative experiments that FantasyStyle outperforms existing state-of-the-art methods in terms of style transfer quality, content preservation, and visual realism. The method is the first to rely solely on diffusion model distillation in the 3DGS style transfer domain.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces several novel elements. The application of frequency-domain analysis and filtering to the latent space of a diffusion model for multi-view consistency is a well-justified and effective approach.  The controllable stylized distillation, particularly the use of negative guidance and the removal of the reconstruction term, is also innovative and directly addresses a specific problem in style transfer. The claim of being the *first* 3DGS style transfer method to rely solely on diffusion distillation is also significant.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses a crucial gap in 3DGS style transfer.  Existing methods often suffer from artifacts, inconsistencies, and content leakage, limiting their practical applications. FantasyStyle's improvements in these areas make it a valuable contribution to the field. The performance improvements in visual quality and faithfulness to the source style are convincing and the method bridges the gap from 2D diffusion based style transfer to 3D scenes. By introducing the MVFC and CSD framework, the authors provide a new avenue for research in 3D style transfer.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear problem definition:** The paper clearly articulates the challenges of multi-view inconsistency and content leakage.
    *   **Well-motivated approach:** The proposed solutions are logically derived from an analysis of the problem.
    *   **Comprehensive experiments:** The qualitative and quantitative evaluations demonstrate the effectiveness of the proposed method. The comparison with existing methods is thorough.
    *   **Strong results:** The visual results presented clearly show the superior performance of FantasyStyle.
    *   **Clarity and Presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to understand, with clear explanations of the methods and experimental setup.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Computational Cost:** The method is acknowledged to be computationally expensive. Further work could explore strategies for improving efficiency. While employing smaller diffusion models is suggested, the actual impact on performance in terms of quality and computational cost needs to be empirically validated.
    *   **Limited Style Control:** While the negative guidance improves content preservation, the degree of user control over specific style attributes could be further enhanced. A way of intuitively controlling the intensity of certain style transfer aspects could be beneficial.
    *   **Domain Specificity:** The work focuses primarily on 3DGS. While the core concepts may be transferable to other 3D representations, this is not explicitly addressed.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of 3D style transfer.  It offers a more robust and visually appealing approach than existing methods and provides a foundation for future research in diffusion-based techniques for 3D content generation and editing. The flexible extension of 2D techniques to 3D domains is a clear advantage.

**Justification for the Score:**

The paper represents a significant advancement in 3DGS style transfer. The proposed method effectively addresses key limitations of existing approaches. While the computational cost and limited style control are drawbacks, the strong results and novel approach justify a high score. The clear presentation and potential impact further contribute to its value.

**Score: 8**

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Can LLMs Detect Their Confabulations? Estimating Reliability in Uncertainty-Aware Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08139v1)
- **Authors**: Tianyi Zhou, Johanne Medina, Sanjay Chawla
- **Abstract**: Large Language Models (LLMs) are prone to generating fluent but incorrect content, known as confabulation, which poses increasing risks in multi-turn or agentic applications where outputs may be reused as context. In this work, we investigate how in-context information influences model behavior and whether LLMs can identify their unreliable responses. We propose a reliability estimation that leverages token-level uncertainty to guide the aggregation of internal model representations. Specifically, we compute aleatoric and epistemic uncertainty from output logits to identify salient tokens and aggregate their hidden states into compact representations for response-level reliability prediction. Through controlled experiments on open QA benchmarks, we find that correct in-context information improves both answer accuracy and model confidence, while misleading context often induces confidently incorrect responses, revealing a misalignment between uncertainty and correctness. Our probing-based method captures these shifts in model behavior and improves the detection of unreliable outputs across multiple open-source LLMs. These results underscore the limitations of direct uncertainty signals and highlight the potential of uncertainty-guided probing for reliability-aware generation.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper investigates the ability of Large Language Models (LLMs) to detect their own confabulations, focusing on how in-context information influences model behavior and response reliability. The authors propose a reliability estimation method that leverages token-level uncertainty to guide the aggregation of internal model representations. They conduct controlled experiments on open Question Answering (QA) benchmarks, manipulating the context to be either correct, incorrect, or absent.  The key findings are that correct context improves accuracy and reduces uncertainty, while misleading context often leads to confidently incorrect responses. The authors then develop probing-based classifiers that use uncertainty-guided token selection from hidden states to improve the detection of unreliable outputs, outperforming direct uncertainty metrics. The work highlights the limitations of direct uncertainty signals and suggests the potential of uncertainty-guided probing for reliability-aware generation.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in its systematic investigation of how context affects LLM confidence and correctness, especially the finding that misleading context can *increase* confidence in incorrect answers.  The probing approach using uncertainty-guided feature selection from hidden states for reliability detection is also a valuable contribution, demonstrating a method to overcome limitations of relying solely on uncertainty scores. While probing techniques are not entirely new, the specific application to detecting confabulations *under the influence of varying contextual information* is a differentiator.  The controlled experimental setup is also a strong aspect, allowing for isolation of context effects.

* **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem: the unreliability of LLMs, particularly in scenarios where they interact with external information. The finding that misleading context can *increase* confidence is particularly significant, as it raises serious concerns about the use of LLMs in agentic settings or multi-turn conversations where previously generated (potentially incorrect) outputs might be used as context. The proposed probing-based reliability detection method offers a practical approach to mitigating this risk, enhancing the safety and trustworthiness of LLMs.  The work is relevant to researchers and practitioners working on LLM safety, retrieval-augmented generation, and agentic AI.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Controlled Experiments:** The rigorous experimental design allows for a clear understanding of the effects of different types of context.
    * **Interesting Findings:** The counterintuitive finding that misleading context can increase confidence is a valuable contribution.
    * **Practical Solution:** The probing-based method offers a practical approach to detecting unreliable outputs.
    * **Multiple Models & Datasets:**  Experiments across several open-source LLMs and QA datasets increase the generalizability of the findings.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Scale of Misleading Context Experiments:** While the controlled setup is beneficial, the manual construction of misleading contexts through ChatGPT is a bottleneck, limiting the scale and diversity of misleading scenarios explored.  Exploring automatically generated adversarial contexts would further strengthen the robustness of their findings.
    * **LLM-as-Judge Limitations:** Reliance on LLM-as-Judge introduces potential biases. Exploring human annotation, even on a smaller subset of examples, would further validate the effectiveness of the proposed method.
    * **Narrow Task Focus:** Focus on QA limits generalizability to other generation tasks like summarization or dialogue.

* **Potential Impact:** The paper's findings and proposed method can influence future research on LLM safety and reliability, particularly in context-aware generation scenarios. It encourages the development of more robust uncertainty estimation techniques and motivates the use of internal model representations for reliability detection. The work could also inform the design of better retrieval-augmented generation systems that are more resilient to noisy or misleading contextual information.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a solid contribution to the field by systematically investigating the impact of contextual information on LLM reliability and proposing a practical detection method. The controlled experimental design and interesting findings justify a high score. However, limitations in the scale of misleading context generation, reliance on LLM-as-judge, and task focus prevent a higher score. The novelty is significant, and the potential impact on the community is substantial, making it a valuable contribution to the field.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Data-Efficient Biomedical In-Context Learning: A Diversity-Enhanced Submodular Perspective
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08140v1)
- **Authors**: Jun Wang, Zaifu Zhan, Qixin Zhang, Mingquan Lin, Meijia Song, Rui Zhang
- **Abstract**: Recent progress in large language models (LLMs) has leveraged their in-context learning (ICL) abilities to enable quick adaptation to unseen biomedical NLP tasks. By incorporating only a few input-output examples into prompts, LLMs can rapidly perform these new tasks. While the impact of these demonstrations on LLM performance has been extensively studied, most existing approaches prioritize representativeness over diversity when selecting examples from large corpora. To address this gap, we propose Dual-Div, a diversity-enhanced data-efficient framework for demonstration selection in biomedical ICL. Dual-Div employs a two-stage retrieval and ranking process: First, it identifies a limited set of candidate examples from a corpus by optimizing both representativeness and diversity (with optional annotation for unlabeled data). Second, it ranks these candidates against test queries to select the most relevant and non-redundant demonstrations. Evaluated on three biomedical NLP tasks (named entity recognition (NER), relation extraction (RE), and text classification (TC)) using LLaMA 3.1 and Qwen 2.5 for inference, along with three retrievers (BGE-Large, BMRetriever, MedCPT), Dual-Div consistently outperforms baselines-achieving up to 5% higher macro-F1 scores-while demonstrating robustness to prompt permutations and class imbalance. Our findings establish that diversity in initial retrieval is more critical than ranking-stage optimization, and limiting demonstrations to 3-5 examples maximizes performance efficiency.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Data-Efficient Biomedical In-Context Learning: A Diversity-Enhanced Submodular Perspective" addresses the challenge of data scarcity in biomedical NLP and its impact on in-context learning (ICL) with large language models (LLMs). The authors propose "Dual-Div," a two-stage framework designed to improve demonstration selection for ICL by optimizing both semantic coverage and diversity. The first stage retrieves a diverse set of candidate examples using submodular optimization, balancing representativeness and diversity. The second stage ranks these candidates conditioned on test queries to select the most relevant and non-redundant demonstrations. The framework is evaluated on biomedical NER, relation extraction, and text classification tasks using Llama 3.1 and Qwen 2.5, and compared with several baselines using different retrievers. The results demonstrate that Dual-Div consistently outperforms existing methods, achieving higher macro-F1 scores while maintaining robustness. The authors find that diversity in the initial retrieval stage is more critical than ranking-stage optimization, and that a limited number of demonstrations (3-5 examples) maximizes performance efficiency.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its systematic integration of diversity metrics into the demonstration selection process for ICL in the biomedical domain. While previous work has considered representativeness or coverage, the explicit and joint optimization of both coverage and diversity using a submodular framework is a valuable contribution. The two-stage approach, separating diversity-enhanced retrieval from conditional ranking, is also a unique aspect of the framework.
*   **Significance:** The paper's findings have significant implications for biomedical NLP, where data scarcity is a common bottleneck. The demonstration that carefully curating diverse examples can significantly improve ICL performance, even with limited data, is a valuable insight. This offers a practical solution to enhance the performance of LLMs on biomedical tasks without extensive fine-tuning or reliance on large, labeled datasets. The study also provides practical guidance, such as limiting demonstrations to 3-5 examples for optimal efficiency.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly identifies the challenge of data scarcity and the importance of demonstration selection in biomedical ICL.
    *   **Well-Defined Methodology:** The Dual-Div framework is well-defined and explained, with clear descriptions of the submodular optimization approach and the two-stage process.
    *   **Comprehensive Experiments:** The experiments are thorough, evaluating the framework on multiple tasks, LLMs, and retrievers, and comparing it against strong baselines.
    *   **Insightful Results:** The results provide valuable insights into the importance of diversity in demonstration selection and the trade-off between quantity and quality of demonstrations.
    *   **Code Clarity:** The writing is clear, making the contributions accessible to the community.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Complexity:** While the methodology is well-defined, the submodular optimization approach can be complex and require a deeper understanding of mathematical concepts. This could limit its accessibility for some researchers.
    *   **Dependency on Semantic Vector Quality:** The performance of Dual-Div relies on the quality of the semantic embedding vectors generated by the retriever models. The framework may be sensitive to the choice of retriever and its ability to capture biomedical knowledge.
    *   **Limited Evaluation of Unlabeled Data Use:** While the paper claims its framework can use unlabeled data in stage 1, this wasn't demonstrated in the results.
    *   **Over Claiming Robustness:** Figure 3 appears to demonstrate that only on the DDI dataset did the algorithm have robustness with respect to other methods. The others show better minimums and medians for Dual-Div, but at the cost of greater variance.
*   **Potential Influence:** The Dual-Div framework has the potential to influence future research in biomedical NLP by providing a practical and effective approach to demonstration selection for ICL. It could be used as a baseline for comparison in future studies and could inspire new approaches to incorporating diversity into ICL.
*   **Score Justification:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to biomedical NLP, with clear methodology, thorough experiments, and insightful results. It effectively addresses the challenge of data scarcity and provides a practical solution for enhancing ICL performance. While the approach has some limitations, such as its complexity and dependency on vector quality, its strengths outweigh its weaknesses.
    The emphasis on diversity is well-justified within the context of biomedical data which is often imbalanced.
    It also makes a compelling argument that diversity should be addressed in stage 1 rather than stage 2.
    Considering its novelty, significance, and potential influence, I assign a score of 7.

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### An effective potential for generative modelling with active matter
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08146v1)
- **Authors**: Adrian Baule
- **Abstract**: Score-based diffusion models generate samples from a complex underlying data distribution by time-reversal of a diffusion process and represent the state-of-the-art in many generative AI applications such as artificial image synthesis. Here, I show how a generative diffusion model can be implemented based on an underlying active particle process with finite correlation time. In contrast to previous approaches that use a score function acting on the velocity coordinate of the active particle, time reversal is here achieved by imposing an effective time-dependent potential on the position coordinate only. The effective potential is valid to first order in the persistence time and leads to a force field that is fully determined by the standard score function and its derivatives up to 2nd order. Numerical experiments for artificial data distributions confirm the validity of the effective potential.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper explores an alternative implementation of generative diffusion models based on active particle dynamics with finite correlation time.  Unlike previous approaches that directly manipulate the velocity coordinate in the time reversal process, this work demonstrates that an equivalent process can be achieved by imposing an effective, time-dependent potential solely on the particle's position coordinate. This potential, valid to first order in the persistence time, is derived and shown to be fully determined by the standard score function and its derivatives. Numerical experiments validate the derived effective potential using artificial data distributions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The novelty of this paper lies in reformulating the time reversal process in generative diffusion models using active matter, *specifically by introducing a position-dependent potential rather than manipulating velocity*. The idea of using active matter for generative modeling isn't entirely new (as the paper itself cites [11]), but *the specific implementation of a position-dependent potential is indeed novel and potentially simplifies experimental realization*. The theoretical derivation using Fox and UCN approximations adds another layer of methodological contribution.
*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from several factors:

    *   **Practical Implications:** The main claim is that the presented formalism may allow for *easier experimental implementation* of generative diffusion models, particularly in systems where directly controlling the particle velocity is challenging. This is a significant potential advantage.
    *   **Theoretical Insight:** The derivation provides *additional theoretical insights into the structure of the time-reversed process* in diffusion models, connecting it to concepts in active matter physics. This deeper understanding could inspire new generative modelling techniques.
    *   **Limited Generalizability:** The authors acknowledge the limitations in validity of the result and in using Fox approximations. The paper doesn't address how the formalism would scale or translate to real-world, high-dimensional datasets (images, etc.). Furthermore, the validity of the method is limited to first order in persistence time and the authors acknowledge the limitations in using the Fox approximations, raising concern on the scope of the proposed formalism.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   *Clear and rigorous derivation*: The paper presents a well-structured and detailed derivation of the effective potential using relevant approximations.
    *   *Connection to Existing Literature*: The authors thoroughly relate their work to previous research on generative diffusion models and active matter.
    *   *Potential for experimental realization*: Provides a potentially simpler approach for experimental implementations of generative AI.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   *Limited Validation*: The numerical experiments are based on simple artificial data distributions in low dimensions. *There is no demonstration of the effectiveness of this approach on more complex, real-world datasets*.
    *   *First-order Approximation*: The validity of the effective potential is limited to first order in the persistence time. This may be a significant limitation in practice.
    *   *Dependence on Approximations*: The derivation relies on specific approximations (Fox and UCN). A theoretical study analyzing the error introduced by these approximations would strengthen the paper.
    *   *Incremental contribution*: While the position-dependent potential is a novel implementation, the performance of the method is limited to the same accuracy of the exact representation Eq. (11) which limits the significance of the finding.

*   **Impact:** The impact is potentially high if the practical advantages are validated. The simplification of the experimental setup could make generative AI more accessible. However, the theoretical approximations and limited scope (first-order) suggest it will probably have limited impact.

**Conclusion:**

The paper presents a novel and interesting approach to implementing generative diffusion models using active matter.  The derivation of the position-dependent effective potential is a valuable contribution, and the potential for simplified experimental realization is promising. However, the limited validation on simple datasets, reliance on approximations, and limited scope somewhat limit the overall significance. While the paper has theoretical merits, its practical impact remains to be demonstrated.

Score: 6.5

- **Classification**: cond-mat.stat-mech
- **Score**: 6/10

### From Natural Language to Solver-Ready Power System Optimization: An LLM-Assisted, Validation-in-the-Loop Framework
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08147v1)
- **Authors**: Yunkai Hu, Tianqiao Zhao, Meng Yue
- **Abstract**: This paper introduces a novel Large Language Models (LLMs)-assisted agent that automatically converts natural-language descriptions of power system optimization scenarios into compact, solver-ready formulations and generates corresponding solutions. In contrast to approaches that rely solely on LLM to produce solutions directly, the proposed method focuses on discovering a mathematically compatible formulation that can be efficiently solved by off-the-shelf optimization solvers. Directly using LLMs to produce solutions often leads to infeasible or suboptimal results, as these models lack the numerical precision and constraint-handling capabilities of established optimization solvers. The pipeline integrates a domain-aware prompt and schema with an LLM, enforces feasibility through systematic validation and iterative repair, and returns both solver-ready models and user-facing results. Using the unit commitment problem as a representative case study, the agent produces optimal or near-optimal schedules along with the associated objective costs. Results demonstrate that coupling the solver with task-specific validation significantly enhances solution reliability. This work shows that combining AI with established optimization frameworks bridges high-level problem descriptions and executable mathematical models, enabling more efficient decision-making in energy systems
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the provided research paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a novel approach to power system optimization by leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) to translate natural language descriptions of optimization scenarios into solver-ready mathematical formulations. Instead of directly using LLMs to generate solutions (which the authors argue is unreliable), they propose an LLM-assisted agent that converts natural language inputs into compact, solver-compatible mixed-integer programs (MIPs). The framework incorporates domain-aware prompting, schema validation, iterative repair mechanisms, and integrates with off-the-shelf optimization solvers like Gurobi. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of their method using the unit commitment (UC) problem as a case study, showing that the agent produces optimal or near-optimal schedules. They further explore solution enhancement techniques such as GNN-guided branching and LLM-based cut separator configuration to reduce solve times.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core novelty lies in the architecture of the LLM-assisted agent specifically designed for translating natural language into formally verifiable and solvable optimization models. While LLMs have been used in power systems and optimization before, the specific approach of using them primarily for formulation *generation* followed by rigorous validation and repair represents a significant departure from methods that directly produce solutions or focus solely on acceleration via ML surrogates. The combination of LLMs for high-level translation, schema validation for ensuring mathematical consistency, and standard solvers for numerical rigor addresses a key limitation of pure LLM-based approaches. The integration of GNN-guided branching adds a layer of efficiency without sacrificing optimality guarantees, demonstrating a well-rounded contribution. The modular approach of solution enhancement is a significant improvement over LLM techniques alone.

*   **Significance:** The significance of this work stems from its potential to bridge the gap between high-level operational policies and executable mathematical models. This is particularly important in the power system domain, where translating evolving policies into accurate and solver-compatible models is often a bottleneck. By automating this process, the proposed framework can enable faster iteration, improve accessibility for non-experts, and enhance the overall decision-making process. Moreover, the validation-in-the-loop approach addresses the inherent limitations of LLMs in numerical precision and constraint handling, paving the way for more reliable and trustworthy AI applications in critical infrastructure.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear and well-defined problem statement.
    *   Novel architecture combining LLMs with traditional optimization solvers and a validation layer.
    *   Rigorous validation and repair mechanisms to ensure solution feasibility.
    *   Demonstration of effectiveness on the unit commitment problem.
    *   Exploration of solution enhancement techniques (GNN branching, LLM-based cuts) for improved efficiency.
    *   Clear experimental setup and results.
    *   Well-written and organized paper.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The case study focuses primarily on the unit commitment problem. While UC is a relevant and representative problem, expanding the evaluation to other power system optimization problems (e.g., economic dispatch, optimal power flow) would further strengthen the claims of generalizability.
    *   While the prompts are shown, a more detailed description of the engineering and thought process behind them could benefit the reader.
    *   While the paper highlights the benefits of using commercial solvers, a more detailed comparison against other open-source solvers could be included.

*   **Potential Influence:**

    The paper has the potential to influence the field by promoting a more principled and reliable approach to integrating AI in power system optimization. The focus on formulation generation, validation, and iterative repair can serve as a blueprint for developing other AI-assisted tools for translating high-level descriptions into executable models in various domains. The GNN-guided branching and LLM-based cuts further showcase a way for classical solvers to be augmented with LLM strategies.

**Score:** 8

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to the field of power system optimization. The integration of LLMs for formulation generation, coupled with rigorous validation and iterative repair, addresses a critical need for bridging the gap between natural language policies and solver-ready models. The results on the unit commitment problem are promising, and the exploration of solution enhancement techniques further enhances the value of the work. The paper is well-written, organized, and clearly demonstrates the effectiveness of the proposed approach.

However, the score is not a 9 or 10 due to the limited scope of the case study and the potential for further improvements in first-pass formulation accuracy and efficiency. Expanding the evaluation to other power system optimization problems and providing a more detailed description of the prompts would further strengthen the claims of generalizability and impact. While valuable, the described enhancements are not a perfect replacement for domain expertise.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 8/10

### REX-RAG: Reasoning Exploration with Policy Correction in Retrieval-Augmented Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08149v1)
- **Authors**: Wentao Jiang, Xiang Feng, Zengmao Wang, Yong Luo, Pingbo Xu, Zhe Chen, Bo Du, Jing Zhang
- **Abstract**: Reinforcement learning (RL) is emerging as a powerful paradigm for enabling large language models (LLMs) to perform complex reasoning tasks. Recent advances indicate that integrating RL with retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) allows LLMs to dynamically incorporate external knowledge, leading to more informed and robust decision making. However, we identify a critical challenge during policy-driven trajectory sampling: LLMs are frequently trapped in unproductive reasoning paths, which we refer to as "dead ends", committing to overconfident yet incorrect conclusions. This severely hampers exploration and undermines effective policy optimization. To address this challenge, we propose REX-RAG (Reasoning Exploration with Policy Correction in Retrieval-Augmented Generation), a novel framework that explores alternative reasoning paths while maintaining rigorous policy learning through principled distributional corrections. Our approach introduces two key innovations: (1) Mixed Sampling Strategy, which combines a novel probe sampling method with exploratory prompts to escape dead ends; and (2) Policy Correction Mechanism, which employs importance sampling to correct distribution shifts induced by mixed sampling, thereby mitigating gradient estimation bias. We evaluate it on seven question-answering benchmarks, and the experimental results show that REX-RAG achieves average performance gains of 5.1% on Qwen2.5-3B and 3.6% on Qwen2.5-7B over strong baselines, demonstrating competitive results across multiple datasets. The code is publicly available at https://github.com/MiliLab/REX-RAG.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "REX-RAG: Reasoning Exploration with Policy Correction in Retrieval-Augmented Generation" addresses the problem of "dead ends" encountered by large language models (LLMs) during reinforcement learning (RL) based training in retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) systems.  Dead ends occur when LLMs get stuck in unproductive reasoning paths, hindering exploration and policy optimization.  The authors propose REX-RAG, which employs a Mixed Sampling Strategy (combining standard policy with exploration via chain-of-thought prompts) and a Policy Correction Mechanism (using importance sampling) to explore alternative reasoning paths while maintaining stable policy learning.  The framework is evaluated on several question-answering benchmarks, demonstrating improved performance compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Novelty:** The paper identifies and formalizes the "dead end" problem, which, while potentially observable in practice, hasn't been explicitly addressed in prior research on RL-based RAG training. The core novelty lies in the combined approach of mixed sampling and policy correction. The use of chain-of-thought prompts for exploration is not entirely new but the adaptive integration with a principled policy correction mechanism using importance sampling makes the combination novel. The framework's design is very good and a great way to improve the training in RAG models.

* **Significance:** The paper is significant because it tackles a practical issue that limits the effectiveness of RL-based RAG training. Overcoming the "dead end" problem can lead to more robust and accurate LLMs in knowledge-intensive tasks. Demonstrating improvements on multiple benchmarks strengthens the impact and broadens the applicability of the approach.  The gains over strong baselines like Search-R1 indicate the practical value of the proposed techniques. It is significant as the work has a positive impact on policy optimization.

* **Strengths:**
    * **Problem Identification:** Clearly defines and demonstrates the "dead end" problem in RAG.
    * **Principled Approach:** The policy correction mechanism is grounded in importance sampling theory, providing a sound foundation for stable training.
    * **Empirical Validation:**  Experiments demonstrate significant improvements over baselines across various benchmarks, supporting the effectiveness of the proposed framework.
    * **Ablation Studies:** Ablation studies confirm the importance of each component within REX-RAG.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Exploration Strategy:** Although the prompt pool approach is easy to implement, it is not adaptive and could potentially limit the scope of exploration compared to more sophisticated methods that generate exploration prompts conditioned on the current reasoning state.
    * **Computational Cost:** The increased sampling and the computation of importance weights add to the computational overhead. Although stated that this is more efficient than uniform oversampling, a more detailed cost analysis would be benefit the community.
    * **Dependency on External Model (GPT-4.5):** REX-RAG is dependent on high-performing models like GPT-4.5 to create the prompt pool which also introduces a cost element that might not scale with all models.

* **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence the design of future RL-based RAG systems by highlighting the importance of exploration strategies and stable policy learning.  The proposed techniques can be readily integrated into existing RL training pipelines. The impact will depend on the community's ability to adapt the code for other LLMs.

* **Overall:** The paper is a very strong contribution. While specific aspects (exploration strategy, cost considerations) could be expanded upon, the core idea and demonstration of effectiveness are significant. It contributes to the field of RAG and reinforcement learning.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and significant contribution to RL-based RAG training by addressing the "dead end" problem with a principled and empirically validated approach. The gains over strong baselines are compelling. However, some limitations exist, including the dependence on a separate model to create the prompts and potential for enhanced exploration strategies, preventing a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### PyVeritas: On Verifying Python via LLM-Based Transpilation and Bounded Model Checking for C
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08171v1)
- **Authors**: Pedro Orvalho, Marta Kwiatkowska
- **Abstract**: Python has become the dominant language for general-purpose programming, yet it lacks robust tools for formal verification. In contrast, programmers working in languages such as C benefit from mature model checkers, for example CBMC, which enable exhaustive symbolic reasoning and fault localisation. The inherent complexity of Python, coupled with the verbosity and low-level nature of existing transpilers (e.g., Cython), have historically limited the applicability of formal verification to Python programs. In this paper, we propose PyVeritas, a novel framework that leverages Large Language Models (LLMs) for high-level transpilation from Python to C, followed by bounded model checking and MaxSAT-based fault localisation in the generated C code. PyVeritas enables verification and bug localisation for Python code using existing model checking tools for C. Our empirical evaluation on two Python benchmarks demonstrates that LLM-based transpilation can achieve a high degree of accuracy, up to 80--90% for some LLMs, enabling effective development environment that supports assertion-based verification and interpretable fault diagnosis for small yet non-trivial Python programs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "PYVERITAS: On Verifying Python via LLM-Based Transpilation and Bounded Model Checking for C."

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PYVERITAS, a novel framework that addresses the lack of robust formal verification tools for Python by leveraging Large Language Models (LLMs) to transpile Python code into C.  The generated C code is then verified using the CBMC bounded model checker. If verification fails, the MaxSAT-based fault localization tool CFAULTS is used to identify potential bug locations in the C code, which are then mapped back to the original Python source.  The authors demonstrate the approach's effectiveness on two Python benchmarks (LIVECODEBENCH and REFACTORY), showing that LLM-based transpilation can be surprisingly accurate, enabling assertion-based verification and fault localization in Python code.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of combining LLM-based transpilation with established C verification tools is novel, though individual components (LLM transpilation, CBMC, CFAULTS) are not. The key innovation lies in intelligently orchestrating these existing tools to solve a significant problem (Python verification). The idea of utilizing LLMs to bridge the gap between a language lacking comprehensive verification tools (Python) and one with mature tools (C) presents a pragmatic and interesting solution. However, the limitations must be accounted for.

*   **Significance:** The significance lies in offering a practical, albeit interim, approach to verifying Python programs, filling a notable void. While native Python model checkers are still under development, PYVERITAS provides a tangible way to analyze and debug Python code using existing, robust C verification technology.  The experiments showing successful verification and fault localization for non-trivial Python examples are encouraging. In a field where formal methods for dynamic languages are challenging, such a practical approach can be a significant contribution. Note that the dependence on LLMs also limits its wide adoption and availability.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Pragmatic Solution:** The paper proposes a realistic solution to a relevant problem.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The experimental evaluation is thorough and well-structured, using established benchmarks and various LLMs.
    *   **Fault Localization:** The integration of MaxSAT-based fault localization adds significant value, providing developers with actionable debugging information.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and easy to follow, clearly explaining the framework and the experimental results.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Reliance on LLMs:**  The framework's accuracy is inherently tied to the performance of the LLM used for transpilation. The LLM may introduce new bugs, or fail on more complex or specialized code, thereby threatening the overall reliability.
    *   **Scalability Concerns:**  While the experiments demonstrate success on small to medium-sized programs, the approach's scalability to larger, more complex Python codebases remains unclear. LLM context length and the computational cost of C verification could become limiting factors.
    *   **Interpretation of Buggy Code:** The reliance on LLMs might be inaccurate when dealing with particularly poorly or obscurely written code.
    *   **Dependency on C-Level Semantics:** Because the Python code is translated to C, the correctness of Python code depends on the translation and C semantics. It's indirect verification.

*   **Potential Influence:**  PYVERITAS could influence future research by demonstrating the potential of combining LLMs with traditional verification techniques. It may also inspire further development of LLM-aware verification tools or more robust Python-to-C transpilers specifically designed for verification.

*   **Justification:** While the paper addresses an important problem in a clever way, the reliance on LLMs as a key component introduces some limitations and potential inaccuracies. The authors acknowledge these limitations and provide a thorough evaluation of several LLMs. This framework provides a pragmatic interim solution. This solution offers benefits such as being able to reuse powerful model checkers for C. There is a tradeoff to be accounted for such as dependence on LLMs and indirect verification. Overall, a rigorous evaluation using a significant number of LLMs justifies that such as framework may be useful under various settings.

**Score: 7**

**Reasoning:** The paper presents a novel and significant approach to Python verification by intelligently combining LLM-based transpilation with existing C verification tools. The thorough evaluation provides convincing evidence of its effectiveness for small to medium-sized programs. Although the approach relies on the accuracy of LLMs, which is an inherent limitation, and scalability is a concern, the pragmatic nature of the solution and the potential influence on future research justify a rating of 7. The inherent complexity of validating and relying on LLMs as the foundation for safety/correctness is a crucial consideration.

- **Classification**: cs.SE
- **Score**: 7/10

### CD-TVD: Contrastive Diffusion for 3D Super-Resolution with Scarce High-Resolution Time-Varying Data
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08173v1)
- **Authors**: Chongke Bi, Xin Gao, Jiangkang Deng, Guan
- **Abstract**: Large-scale scientific simulations require significant resources to generate high-resolution time-varying data (TVD). While super-resolution is an efficient post-processing strategy to reduce costs, existing methods rely on a large amount of HR training data, limiting their applicability to diverse simulation scenarios. To address this constraint, we proposed CD-TVD, a novel framework that combines contrastive learning and an improved diffusion-based super-resolution model to achieve accurate 3D super-resolution from limited time-step high-resolution data. During pre-training on historical simulation data, the contrastive encoder and diffusion superresolution modules learn degradation patterns and detailed features of high-resolution and low-resolution samples. In the training phase, the improved diffusion model with a local attention mechanism is fine-tuned using only one newly generated high-resolution timestep, leveraging the degradation knowledge learned by the encoder. This design minimizes the reliance on large-scale high-resolution datasets while maintaining the capability to recover fine-grained details. Experimental results on fluid and atmospheric simulation datasets confirm that CD-TVD delivers accurate and resource-efficient 3D super-resolution, marking a significant advancement in data augmentation for large-scale scientific simulations. The code is available at https://github.com/Xin-Gao-private/CD-TVD.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CD-TVD: Contrastive Diffusion for 3D Super-Resolution with Scarce High-Resolution Time-Varying Data":

**Summary:**

The paper presents CD-TVD, a novel deep learning framework for 3D super-resolution of time-varying data (TVD) in scientific simulations. The key innovation is to combine contrastive learning with a diffusion-based super-resolution model to address the challenge of scarce high-resolution (HR) training data. The framework operates in two stages: (1) pre-training on historical simulation data to learn HR-LR degradation patterns using contrastive learning and (2) fine-tuning the diffusion model with limited HR data from a new scenario.  A local attention mechanism is integrated into the diffusion model to improve efficiency and detail recovery. The authors demonstrate the effectiveness of CD-TVD on fluid and atmospheric simulation datasets, showing improved accuracy and resource efficiency compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper demonstrates novelty in several aspects:
    *   **Contrastive Learning for Degradation Modeling:** Applying contrastive learning to explicitly model the degradation patterns between HR and LR scientific data is a novel approach. This allows the model to learn discriminative features that are robust to unseen data distributions.
    *   **Diffusion Model with Local Attention:** Integrating a local attention mechanism into a diffusion-based super-resolution network is innovative. This adaptation addresses the high computational costs associated with diffusion models, making them more practical for 3D scientific data.
    *   **Two-Stage Training with Scarce HR Data:** The two-stage pre-training and fine-tuning approach is a significant contribution. It enables the model to generalize well to new scenarios with only a small amount of HR data, which is a major limitation of existing methods.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial challenge in scientific visualization: the limited availability of high-resolution simulation data. CD-TVD has the potential to significantly reduce the computational cost of generating high-resolution TVD, enabling scientists to explore more complex simulations and improve the accuracy of their analyses.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Effective Combination of Techniques:** The integration of contrastive learning and diffusion models is well-motivated and leads to improved performance.
    *   **Resource Efficiency:** The use of local attention and the ability to fine-tune with limited HR data make CD-TVD more resource-efficient than existing methods.
    *   **Strong Empirical Results:** The experimental results on diverse datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of CD-TVD in capturing complex flow dynamics and reconstructing fine-grained details.
    *   **Clear and Well-Written:** The paper is well-structured and clearly explains the technical details of the proposed method.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Sensitivity to Dataset Similarity:** While the method is designed to handle scarce HR data, the performance might be sensitive to the similarity between the pre-training and fine-tuning datasets. This is acknowledged by the authors. More analysis is needed to understand the limitations and robustness in significantly different datasets.
    *   **Spatial-Only Super-resolution:** The current framework only addresses spatial super-resolution and doesn't integrate temporal super-resolution, which limits its applicability to certain types of datasets.
    *   **End-to-End Training:** Lack of end-to-end training.

*   **Potential Impact:** CD-TVD has the potential to become a widely used technique for super-resolution in scientific visualization, particularly in fields where generating high-resolution data is computationally expensive. The contrastive learning component could be adapted to other data augmentation or generation tasks.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper presents a novel and well-executed approach to a significant problem in scientific visualization. The combination of contrastive learning and diffusion models, along with the local attention mechanism, is a strong technical contribution. The empirical results demonstrate the effectiveness and resource efficiency of the proposed method.  However, there are limitations, such as the dependence on dataset similarity and lack of temporal integration, which prevent it from reaching a higher score. It is a significant and novel approach, but requires further development to broaden its applicability.

### MedReasoner: Reinforcement Learning Drives Reasoning Grounding from Clinical Thought to Pixel-Level Precision
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08177v1)
- **Authors**: Zhonghao Yan, Muxi Diao, Yuxuan Yang, Jiayuan Xu, Kaizhou Zhang, Ruoyan Jing, Lele Yang, Yanxi Liu, Kongming Liang, Zhanyu Ma
- **Abstract**: Accurately grounding regions of interest (ROIs) is critical for diagnosis and treatment planning in medical imaging. While multimodal large language models (MLLMs) combine visual perception with natural language, current medical-grounding pipelines still rely on supervised fine-tuning with explicit spatial hints, making them ill-equipped to handle the implicit queries common in clinical practice. This work makes three core contributions. We first define Unified Medical Reasoning Grounding (UMRG), a novel vision-language task that demands clinical reasoning and pixel-level grounding. Second, we release U-MRG-14K, a dataset of 14K samples featuring pixel-level masks alongside implicit clinical queries and reasoning traces, spanning 10 modalities, 15 super-categories, and 108 specific categories. Finally, we introduce MedReasoner, a modular framework that distinctly separates reasoning from segmentation: an MLLM reasoner is optimized with reinforcement learning, while a frozen segmentation expert converts spatial prompts into masks, with alignment achieved through format and accuracy rewards. MedReasoner achieves state-of-the-art performance on U-MRG-14K and demonstrates strong generalization to unseen clinical queries, underscoring the significant promise of reinforcement learning for interpretable medical grounding.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new vision-language task called Unified Medical Reasoning Grounding (UMRG), designed to address the challenge of grounding clinical thoughts to precise pixel-level regions of interest (ROIs) in medical images when the queries are implicit or require reasoning. To facilitate this, the authors create and release U-MRG-14K, a dataset of 14K samples consisting of pixel-level masks, implicit clinical queries, and chain-of-thought (CoT) reasoning traces across various medical imaging modalities and anatomical categories. The paper also presents MedReasoner, a modular framework that separates reasoning from segmentation. MedReasoner employs a reinforcement learning (RL) approach to optimize an MLLM reasoner, while leveraging a frozen segmentation expert to convert spatial prompts into masks.  Experiments show that MedReasoner achieves state-of-the-art performance on U-MRG-14K and generalizes well to unseen clinical queries.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies in several aspects:

    *   **Task Formulation:** The UMRG task itself is a valuable contribution.  It more closely reflects real-world clinical workflows where clinicians often formulate implicit queries and need to identify specific regions of interest. Current VQA datasets are limited to explicit queries and do not focus on spatial grounding.
    *   **Dataset Creation:** The U-MRG-14K dataset is a significant resource for the community. It addresses the lack of datasets with both implicit queries and pixel-level annotations. The use of GPT-4o to simulate clinician behavior and generate semantically rich questions is innovative.
    *   **Framework Design:**  MedReasoner's modular design, which decouples reasoning and segmentation, is an interesting architectural choice. This plug-and-play approach allows for easy substitution and upgrading of either component. The use of RL to train the reasoning module without relying on expensive annotated CoT traces is also a positive point.

*   **Significance:** The paper addresses a crucial problem in medical imaging: enabling models to understand and respond to implicit clinical queries with precise spatial grounding. The introduction of UMRG and U-MRG-14K allows for better benchmarks and the development of more clinically relevant grounding pipelines.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The rigorous dataset construction process using a three-stage prompting pipeline with manual verification.
    *   The use of reinforcement learning to address the annotation hunger and phrase overfitting issues inherent in supervised fine-tuning.
    *   The modular design of MedReasoner allows for flexible improvements in reasoning and segmentation aspects without complete retraining.
    *   The extensive experimental results that demonstrate MedReasoner's superiority over existing methods and strong generalization capabilities.
    *   The release of code and the dataset promotes reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   While the paper emphasizes the ability to handle *implicit* queries, the precise definition of 'implicit' could be made more explicit. Further clarification on the spectrum of implicitness (vs. explicit) of questions would strengthen the characterization of UMRG and U-MRG-14K.  The distinction between different types of implicitness in clinical questions would also be useful.
    *   Although RL is used, the reward function design may seem ad-hoc. A more systematic approach to reward engineering or an exploration of alternative RL algorithms might further enhance the performance and robustness of the framework.
    *   The reliance on MedSAM2 as the default ASM means that the method is to some extent, constrained by MedSAM2 performance. Exploring other ASM possibilities could have provided a more complete benchmark.
    *   The evaluation of different modalities is not explicitly presented. The breakdown in performance for unseen query patterns over different modalities would have been interesting.
    *   While impressive, the RL setup is still rule-based and has a cost (albeit lower) attached to the generation of pseudo labels. The results would have been strengthened by the inclusion of an unsupervised technique for comparison.

*   **Potential Impact:** This work has the potential to significantly impact the field of medical image analysis by enabling the development of more interpretable and clinically useful AI systems. The UMRG task and U-MRG-14K dataset can serve as benchmarks for future research in reasoning-based medical image grounding. It also paves the way for more effective human-AI collaboration in clinical settings.

*   **Justification for Score:** While the paper presents a novel task and dataset with a well-designed framework, there are some limitations in the evaluation, the RL setup, and the definition of implicitness, as noted above. Also the dataset is built upon data from older datasets and the core GPT-4o, thus limiting the overall novelty.
*
*   I think the work offers a promising step forward and its impact will be significant, a few limitations temper its true significance.

### THAT: Token-wise High-frequency Augmentation Transformer for Hyperspectral Pansharpening
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08183v1)
- **Authors**: Hongkun Jin, Hongcheng Jiang, Zejun Zhang, Yuan Zhang, Jia Fu, Tingfeng Li, Kai Luo
- **Abstract**: Transformer-based methods have demonstrated strong potential in hyperspectral pansharpening by modeling long-range dependencies. However, their effectiveness is often limited by redundant token representations and a lack of multi-scale feature modeling. Hyperspectral images exhibit intrinsic spectral priors (e.g., abundance sparsity) and spatial priors (e.g., non-local similarity), which are critical for accurate reconstruction. From a spectral-spatial perspective, Vision Transformers (ViTs) face two major limitations: they struggle to preserve high-frequency components--such as material edges and texture transitions--and suffer from attention dispersion across redundant tokens. These issues stem from the global self-attention mechanism, which tends to dilute high-frequency signals and overlook localized details. To address these challenges, we propose the Token-wise High-frequency Augmentation Transformer (THAT), a novel framework designed to enhance hyperspectral pansharpening through improved high-frequency feature representation and token selection. Specifically, THAT introduces: (1) Pivotal Token Selective Attention (PTSA) to prioritize informative tokens and suppress redundancy; (2) a Multi-level Variance-aware Feed-forward Network (MVFN) to enhance high-frequency detail learning. Experiments on standard benchmarks show that THAT achieves state-of-the-art performance with improved reconstruction quality and efficiency. The source code is available at https://github.com/kailuo93/THAT.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "THAT: Token-wise High-frequency Augmentation Transformer for Hyperspectral Pansharpening" introduces a new transformer-based architecture for hyperspectral pansharpening. The core idea is to improve the reconstruction of high-frequency components and reduce redundancy in token representations, which the authors argue are limitations of existing transformer-based approaches.  The proposed "Token-wise High-frequency Augmentation Transformer (THAT)" utilizes two key modules: (1) Pivotal Token Selective Attention (PTSA) to prioritize informative tokens and suppress redundancy through k-means clustering, and (2) a Multi-level Variance-aware Feed-forward Network (MVFN) to enhance high-frequency detail learning by capturing hierarchical spectral-spatial dependencies using depthwise convolutions and variance modeling. The authors demonstrate state-of-the-art performance on several hyperspectral datasets, showing improvements in PSNR, SSIM, SAM, ERGAS, and SCC metrics compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Addresses a relevant problem:** Hyperspectral pansharpening is a crucial task in remote sensing, and improving the preservation of high-frequency details is important for accurate analysis and interpretation of the data.
*   **Novel architecture:**  The proposed architecture introduces PTSA and MVFN, which are designed to address specific shortcomings of existing transformer-based approaches. PTSA appears to be a computationally efficient method for token selection. The MVFN module, which uses variance information, is a good attempt to model high frequency components.
*   **Strong experimental results:** The paper presents compelling experimental results on standard benchmarks, demonstrating that the proposed THAT architecture outperforms several state-of-the-art methods. Ablation studies provide evidence for the contribution of each module (PTSA and MVFN).
*   **Clear presentation:** The paper is generally well-written and easy to follow. The architecture is clearly described with supporting figures, and the experimental setup is well-defined.
*   **Source Code Availability:** The availability of source code is a significant plus, as it promotes reproducibility and allows other researchers to build upon this work.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Incremental Novelty:** While the architecture is novel in its specific combination of components, the individual ideas are somewhat incremental.  K-means clustering for token selection and multi-scale feature extraction are well-established techniques. The novelty hinges on the specific application and combination.
*   **Limited Theoretical Justification:** The paper lacks a strong theoretical analysis of why PTSA and MVFN are effective. Although the ablation studies demonstrate their contribution, a more in-depth explanation of the underlying mechanisms would be beneficial. The choice of k-means clustering and the specific architecture of MVFN appears somewhat ad-hoc.
*   **Computational Complexity:** While the authors compare FLOPs, a more detailed analysis of the computational complexity and memory requirements is needed, particularly concerning the scalability of the k-means clustering used in PTSA to very large images. The comparison to PLRDiff is not entirely informative as it is significantly more computationally expensive.
*   **Limited Evaluation of Generalization:** The evaluation is primarily on a few standard datasets. The paper could benefit from including results on more diverse and challenging datasets to assess the generalization capability of the proposed method.

**Significance:**

The paper contributes to the field of hyperspectral pansharpening by proposing a new transformer-based architecture that improves the preservation of high-frequency details and reduces token redundancy. The strong experimental results suggest that the proposed THAT architecture is a promising approach for this task.  However, the incremental nature of the novelty and the lack of deeper theoretical insights somewhat limit the paper's overall impact. The efficiency of the algorithm also needs a more rigourous evaluation.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:**

The paper presents a solid contribution to the field. The proposed architecture is effective and achieves state-of-the-art results. The source code availability and strong experimental results enhance its practical value. However, the novelty is somewhat incremental, and the theoretical justification could be strengthened. A more comprehensive complexity analysis and generalization assessment are also desirable. The paper pushes the state-of-the-art and provides a useful architecture for hyperspectral pansharpening, but the contributions are not transformative enough to warrant a higher score.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### Reinforcement Learning in Vision: A Survey
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2508.08189v1)
- **Authors**: Weijia Wu, Chen Gao, Joya Chen, Kevin Qinghong Lin, Qingwei Meng, Yiming Zhang, Yuke Qiu, Hong Zhou, Mike Zheng Shou
- **Abstract**: Recent advances at the intersection of reinforcement learning (RL) and visual intelligence have enabled agents that not only perceive complex visual scenes but also reason, generate, and act within them. This survey offers a critical and up-to-date synthesis of the field. We first formalize visual RL problems and trace the evolution of policy-optimization strategies from RLHF to verifiable reward paradigms, and from Proximal Policy Optimization to Group Relative Policy Optimization. We then organize more than 200 representative works into four thematic pillars: multi-modal large language models, visual generation, unified model frameworks, and vision-language-action models. For each pillar we examine algorithmic design, reward engineering, benchmark progress, and we distill trends such as curriculum-driven training, preference-aligned diffusion, and unified reward modeling. Finally, we review evaluation protocols spanning set-level fidelity, sample-level preference, and state-level stability, and we identify open challenges that include sample efficiency, generalization, and safe deployment. Our goal is to provide researchers and practitioners with a coherent map of the rapidly expanding landscape of visual RL and to highlight promising directions for future inquiry. Resources are available at: https://github.com/weijiawu/Awesome-Visual-Reinforcement-Learning.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper, including a novelty/significance score.

**Summary:**

This paper presents a survey of the rapidly evolving field of visual reinforcement learning (RL). It covers recent advances in integrating RL techniques with large multimodal models, visual generation models, unified frameworks, and vision-language-action agents. The survey traces the development of policy optimization strategies, from RLHF and Proximal Policy Optimization to Group Relative Policy Optimization, and organizes over 200 representative works into four thematic areas. The authors delve into algorithmic design, reward engineering, benchmark progress, and evaluation protocols. Finally, the paper identifies open challenges like sample efficiency, generalization, and safe deployment, aiming to provide researchers and practitioners with a comprehensive map of the field and to highlight future research directions.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Comprehensiveness:** The survey aims for breadth, covering a substantial number of recent publications. The organization into thematic pillars is helpful for navigating the field.
*   **Timeliness:** The focus on the surge in research activity since 2024 makes the survey timely and relevant. Including Gemini 2.5 (DeepMind, 2025) shows effort to include the most recent developments.
*   **Clear Structure:** The division into algorithmic design, reward engineering, and benchmark progress for each pillar provides a consistent framework for analysis.
*   **Practical Focus:** The identification of open challenges and future directions is useful for researchers seeking to contribute to the field.
*   **Github repository for resources:** This is excellent for practical applications and exploration of the papers and concepts discussed.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Depth of Analysis:** Covering over 200 papers implies a potential trade-off between breadth and depth. It is possible that the analysis of individual papers might be somewhat superficial due to the sheer volume of material covered. A more selective approach that focuses on a smaller number of seminal works with in-depth analysis may provide a more substantial contribution.
*   **Critical Perspective:** While the survey synthesizes the field, a more critical perspective on the limitations of current approaches and the potential pitfalls of specific techniques could have been added.  The section about challenges are important but could be more detailed.
*   **Novelty of Taxonomy:** The taxonomy should have been more precisely defined to distinguish between the categories, which are slightly overlapping.  For instance, a unified model is both multi-modal and may involve action.
*   **Timeline Representation:** Figure 1, representing the timeline, is not very informative. The models are grouped by "Multimodal LLM," "Visual Gen," "Unified Model," and "VLA Model", with overlapping purposes, which makes it difficult to grasp the timeline. It may be better to group by the date and model purpose instead of fixed grouping.
*   **Lack of Discussion of Ethical Implications:** The document does not mention or discuss ethical implications.
*   **Clarity in Definitions:** There could be more explicit definitions of key concepts and terminology used throughout the survey. This would make the survey more accessible to readers who are new to the field.

**Significance:**

The survey fills a need for a comprehensive overview of visual RL, especially its recent integration with large models. It can be valuable for researchers entering the field, practitioners seeking to apply visual RL techniques, and anyone wanting to stay up-to-date on the latest developments.  However, given the number of similar surveys emerging rapidly, the long-term significance hinges on how thoroughly it is updated and the depth of critical insights it provides over time.

**Justification for Score:**

The survey is well-structured, provides a broad overview of the field, and is timely. However, it sacrifices some depth of analysis and critical perspective for comprehensiveness. The taxonomy could also be refined for better clarity. While the survey is a valuable resource, it lacks some important dimensions to take it to the highest tier of impact.
